# Design decisions

Every non-obvious choice, with the reasoning behind it. Newest constraints first, then by subsystem.
The decision logs are records of their sessions and are not rewritten: where a log names revision A2 boards or A2
values, the Revision A3 log says what changed (the superseded A2 decisions are listed at the top of the A2 log), and
the numbered sections 0 to 11 and the Open items describe the board as it is now, revision A3.
"Verified against datasheet" means checked against the manufacturer document named in [section 9](#9-footprint--pin-out-verification-against-the-datasheets);
anything that could not be verified is called out in [Open items](#open-items---things-a-human-must-re-check).

State on 2026-11-21, after review round 2 of revision A3 (the last round): the board is revision A3 and is
generated with KiCad 10.0.8 (`hardware/build.sh`, project image `voxhub-kicad:10.0`); the generated reports of that
day show ERC 0 errors and 0 warnings and DRC 0 violations, 0 unconnected pads and 0 footprint errors. A guard keeps
the generating build steps from overwriting project files that were edited in KiCad (log K-11, R-8 to R-18 and R-38
to R-43, section 6a). The default microphone gain of the VoxHub Assistant add-on is 32 (log R-27), and the test suite
has 144 tests, 93 for the VoxHub Assistant add-on and 51 for the VoxHub Satellite add-on. Stock and prices were read
twice, in the read of 2026-11-20 and in the read of 2026-11-21, 09:57 UTC (log, "Final stock and price check");
every price and total of the current-state sections comes from the second. No board has been built. Every figure in
this document is calculated, simulated, read from a data sheet or measured in the generated files; nothing was
tested on hardware or on Windows, and where windows of KiCad were needed they ran on a virtual display on Linux and
were operated by a script, not by a person.

## Revision A3 (fifth session, 2026-11-20): cheaper order, KiCad 10, final check - decision log

Goal of this session: the owner orders soon. Make the JLCPCB order cheaper, move the project and all build scripts to
KiCad 10 so that it can be opened, edited and saved in KiCad 10 on Windows, check stock and prices of every BOM line
once more, keep the hub / satellite software working. Rules of the owner: never change a value that sets a voltage, a
current limit, a timing or a strap configuration without recalculating and verifying it; ERC and DRC stay at zero; no
questions; no comments added to code or config files; independent reviews of the changed areas, at most two rounds;
commit and push after every milestone.

The session went on into 2026-11-21 with the local-editing package (milestone 3, K-11 to K-15) and review round 1,
which has two parts (R-1 to R-7, then R-8 to R-37); all three are at the end of this log. Review round 1 corrected
several entries of the tables above it, in its second part also K-6, K-7 and K-11 to K-14. Those entries were
corrected in place under their old identifier and say so.

### KiCad 10 migration (milestone 1: the same board, built by KiCad 10 tooling, stored in native KiCad 10 files)

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| K-1 | **Toolchain: KiCad 10.0.8.** The build runs in the project image `voxhub-kicad:10.0`, which `hardware/build.sh` builds itself from `hardware/docker/Dockerfile` when it is missing (official `kicad/kicad:10.0` plus `python3-numpy` and `python3-shapely` from the Debian packages). `KICAD_NATIVE=1` still works and now refuses a `kicad-cli` that is not version 10 | The official image has the `pcbnew` Python module but neither numpy nor shapely nor pip, so the old "pip install shapely inside the container" fallback could never work. One image, one version: the generators support KiCad 10 only, there are no fallbacks to the KiCad 9 API |
| K-2 | **The generators write native KiCad 10 files:** schematic format 20260306, symbol library 20251024, footprints and board 20260206, project file with the key set of a fresh KiCad 10 project, library tables in KiCad 10's layout. `gen/sexpr.py` reproduces KiCad's own file layout | Tested: a forced `kicad-cli sym / fp / pcb upgrade` rewrites nothing, `sch upgrade --force` rewrites one line of the root sheet (`embedded_fonts`, written here the way the KiCad 10 editor saves it), loading and saving the board with pcbnew rewrites 0 of 67,318 lines, and an open-edit-save in both KiCad 10 editors (Linux, under Xvfb) left every tracked file byte-identical with no conversion notice. Two more saves from the editor windows in review round 1, again under Xvfb, gave the same result; the guard of milestone 3 accepts the root sheet with and without the `embedded_fonts` line (R-13) |
| K-3 | **The project carries its own copies of every stock symbol and footprint it uses** (`hardware/gen/stock/`: the KiCad 9.0.9 library content in KiCad 10 format, 33 symbols and 30 footprints); `make_footprints.py` copies the used ones into `voxhub.pretty`, every footprint link is `voxhub:<name>`, and the generators no longer read `/usr/share/kicad` | The KiCad 10.0.8 standard library is not the one the board was designed with: 7 of the 30 footprints differ (shield pads renamed S1 / 9 / 11 to SH on USB-C, USB-A and microSD, USB-A pin 1 rectangle to rounded rectangle, the pin-1 silkscreen marker of the three QFN footprints, pad properties) and 4 of the 33 symbols. Building against it would change netlist, copper and silkscreen. Side effect wanted for Windows: the project opens without depending on the global library tables of the installation. A part that is not in `gen/stock` must be added there before the generator can use it |
| K-4 | **API and command-line changes ported:** `FOOTPRINT.GetFieldByName` / `HasFieldByName` became `GetField` / `HasField`, `ZONE.SetDoNotAllowCopperPour` became `SetDoNotAllowZoneFills`; `kicad-cli pcb export pdf --mode-multipage -o` now takes the PDF file, not a directory. All other options of the build exist unchanged, the Gerber / drill options are still the ones JLCPCB documents | All 52 module-level names and 137 methods the scripts use were compared between the KiCad 9 and KiCad 10 `pcbnew` modules |
| K-5 | **New checks of KiCad 10 are errors like the old ones** (6 DRC ids, 6 ERC ids). Two fired on the unchanged A2 board and were fixed at the cause: `footprint_symbol_field_mismatch` (150: footprints now carry the hidden Datasheet / Description / MPN / Manufacturer fields of their symbols) and `track_not_centered_on_via` (1: a 0.33 um offset of one autorouted GND stub, now snapped onto the via) | A warning that is always there hides the next one. The severity of every key that existed in A2 is unchanged; the two library-comparison checks stay waived and are reported in `outputs/drc_waived_checks_enabled.rpt` (now 4 warnings: U2 type, J4, J6, C13). Milestone 3 went further: every check is an error except four ignored keys (K-14) |
| K-6 | **Three land patterns changed, by copper, not by a waiver** (two in milestone 1, the third in review round 1). KiCad 10 tests pad-to-hole clearance in both directions of a pad pair, KiCad 9 tested one; 8 violations of the 0.2 mm hole-to-copper limit appeared that had been in the land patterns all along: the ring land of the Wurth SMT spacers H5-H8 touched its own 3.7 mm non-plated hole (0.0 mm), and the outer contacts A1 / B12 / A12 / B1 of the USB-C receptacle J1 were 0.194 mm from the 0.65 mm locating-peg holes. `make_footprints.py` (`LAND_FIXES`) now writes the spacer ring from 2.10 to 3.00 mm radius (was 1.85 to 3.00 mm; 0.25 mm from the hole) and the four USB-C contacts 1.11 mm long (was 1.15 mm, pad centres unchanged; 0.21 mm from the hole). The third land pattern of this kind was not reported by the DRC and was found on 2026-11-21 by the order-guide check (R-2): the ground ring of the microphones MK1 / MK2 started 0.18 mm from the edge of its own 0.8 mm non-plated sound hole. `LAND_FIXES` now writes that ring from 0.61 to 1.04 mm radius (was 0.58 to 1.04 mm; 0.43 mm wide instead of 0.46 mm; 0.21 mm from the hole) | JLCPCB removes copper that is closer than 0.2 mm to a non-plated hole in its own data preparation, so the delivered boards are the same either way; now the Gerbers say what is built and the DRC is clean without an exception. The spacer keeps 82 % of its land area. Its paste apertures were left as they were at this point, and so were those of the microphone ring. That was a fault, found in the second part of review round 1 (OP-1): 16 % of the spacer paste lay on solder mask. Since then `LAND_FIXES` moves the paste as well: at the spacer it starts at 2.15 mm radius (10.22 mm2 per spacer, 80 % of before), at the microphone ring at 0.63 mm (R-19). KiCad's DRC does not test the microphone ring pad against the hole of its own footprint; the reason was not investigated. With the third change no copper of the board is closer than 0.2 mm to a non-plated hole |
| K-7 | **Proof that it is the same board:** a geometric comparison of the A2 board file with the rebuilt one (pcbnew + shapely, nanometre exact): 150 footprints with positions, rotations and sides, 764 pad-to-net assignments, 1384 track segments, 286 vias, 77 zones and rule areas, 330 drill hits, outline and silkscreen are identical. Different: the eight pads of K-6, one track end by 0.33 um (K-5), the footprint library nickname (K-3), hidden fields (K-5), and 0.28 mm2 of 4190 mm2 zone fill on the component side (KiCad 10's filler leaves out one thermal spoke and one stub at J1 that lie under a locked track; the rest are slivers below 0.003 mm2). BOM, CPL, placement report and the four gate reports are byte-identical to A2. An independent comparison of the A3 Gerber files with those of A2 in review round 1 (OP-3, R-21) found what this row lists and two things it does not list: refill differences of at most 0.03 mm at six places of the top ground fill (0.0009 to 0.0019 mm2 each), and a smaller corner radius of the 200 paste apertures of the CM4 sockets J2 / J3 (0.035 instead of 0.0425 mm, size 0.17 x 0.67 mm unchanged). No gap and no connection differs | The board file and the Gerbers are not byte-reproducible (KiCad writes items in the order of fresh random identifiers), so only a geometric comparison can show that nothing moved |
| K-8 | **`postroute.sh` counts only violations that name a track or a via as repairable**; the final DRC gate of `build.sh outputs` is unchanged and strict | The repair step can remove nothing but tracks and vias; with a pad-only violation in the list the loop never converged |
| K-9 | **`.gitattributes` keeps KiCad files, scripts, Gerber and drill files at LF line endings on a Windows checkout** and marks the binary outputs; `.gitignore` covers what KiCad 10 creates beside an opened project (`.history/`, `*-bak`, `_autosave-*`, lock files, local settings). CSV files are left as generated (CRLF from Python's csv module) | A Windows checkout with automatic line-ending conversion must not rewrite the project or break the shell scripts inside the Linux container |
| K-10 | **Not verified in this session:** anything on Windows itself (the evidence is KiCad 10.0.8 on Linux, including both editors), `build.sh` under Git Bash / Docker Desktop, a fresh autoroute without `SKIP_ROUTE=1` (KiCad 10 writes the Specctra DSN file differently; the committed session imports unchanged), a `docker build` without this sandbox's cache | No Windows machine and no display here. First opening on Windows: KiCad's own setup wizard appears once. At this milestone the Plot dialog defaults were wrong for the order (no Protel extensions, solder mask not subtracted from silkscreen); since milestone 3 the board file stores the plot settings of `build.sh`, the drill dialog still has none (K-14) |

### Cost reduction: fewer part numbers (milestone 2, hardware revision A3)

Every changed value was checked against the manufacturer's data sheet by an independent agent before it went into
`gen/design.py` (documents fetched on 2026-11-20: Infineon CYPD3177 002-25383 Rev. *C and the CY4533 kit guide
002-25263 Rev. *B, AOS AO3401A Rev 3.1, onsemi MM3Z Rev. 15, TI LMR33640 SNVSB99C, Microchip USB251xB DS00001692E,
TI TPS2553 SLVS841G, Infineon IM69D130 V1.0 / IM72D128 V1.01 / IM73D122 V1.00, Analog Devices ADAU7002 Rev. C and
MAX98357A, the KENTO LED sheets A.0, Raspberry Pi CM4 data sheet release 4, USB Type-C R2.5 and USB PD R3.3). Copper,
placement and nets are unchanged: the A3 board differs from the KiCad 10 build of A2 only in values, part numbers and
the revision text on the silkscreen (geometric comparison: pads, 1384 tracks, 286 vias, zone fills identical).
Two additions from review round 1. The VBUS_MIN divider R1 / R2 = 12k / 2.2k had no stored independent check when it
was built; the reviewer made it (C-2). And one value and two pads changed after this milestone: R41 (R-1) and the
ground rings of the two microphones (R-2); tracks, vias, zones and drill hits are still those of A2. In the second
part of that round the paste apertures of six parts followed their narrowed lands (the spacers H5-H8 and the two
microphones, R-19), and the board file got the title block of the schematic (R-24).

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| C-1 | **What counts is the number of distinct JLCPCB part numbers, not Basic against Extended.** The BOM goes from 53 to 44 lines (resistor values 15 to 9, capacitor lines 11 to 9, LED lines 4 to 3), the machine-placed part types from 51 to 42 | JLCPCB's price list of 22 Oct 2026: "Feeder Loading: $1.53 Basic/Extend" for Standard PCBA - "all basic and extended components require feeder loading". A Basic part saves nothing in this order; 9 feeders less are 13.77 USD |
| C-2 | **PD strap resistors (CYPD3177):** R3 (VBUS_MAX to VDDD) and R6 (ISNK_FINE to ground) are 1k instead of 0 ohm; ISNK_COARSE = 2 A is set with R4 / R5 = 2.2k / 1k instead of 5.1k / 2.4k; VBUS_MIN = 9 V with R1 / R2 = 12k / 2.2k instead of 5.1k / 1k. The strap meaning is unchanged: 9 V minimum, 20 V maximum, 2 A, +0 mA. R8 = 49.9k on FLIP stays | The data sheet publishes the pin voltage window of every strap level (0-248 / 249-786 / 787-1347 / 1348-1920 / 1921-2778 / from 2779 mV at 3.3 V, read by an 8-bit ADC with about 4 LSB = 52 mV total error); the resistor values in its tables are "suggested". With 1 % resistors: VBUS_MIN 503-520 mV in 249-786 (the old values gave 532-550 mV; the window centre is 517 mV), ISNK_COARSE 1017-1046 mV in 787-1347 (smallest margin 230 mV = 17.8 LSB), a single 1k moves an end level by 2 uV (2 nA leakage). Infineon's own kit sets end levels with single resistors. The dividers draw 1.26 mA from VDDD continuously instead of 0.98 mA; while FAULT is high it is about 2.7 mA, because the red LED D4 is fed from the FAULT pin through R7 = 1k (as in A2; the rating of the internal 3.3 V regulator is not published - for a next revision feed D4 from VBUS through a FET as Infineon's kit does). FLIP reads its pull-up value: 50k means "not data capable", which is what this power-only port is - it must not be merged into a small value. Lines saved: 0 ohm, 2.4k, 5.1k. Review round 1 (R-6): the stored pre-check of this block had evaluated VBUS_MIN with the old 5.1k / 1k; the reviewer recomputed the 12k / 2.2k of the board on its own: 511 mV (503 to 520 mV) in the 249 to 786 mV window, margins 254 / 266 mV |
| C-3 | **Gate network of the load switch Q1:** R10 = 100k instead of 150k (R9 stays 100k), so VGS = -0.5 x VBUS instead of -0.4 x VBUS; C7 = 100 nF 16 V instead of 10 nF 50 V. The 10 V Zener D5 is now a working part of the circuit, not only a transient clamp | VGS is 3.56 V at the lowest valid sink voltage of a 9 V contract (7.30 V), 4.4-4.5 V at exactly 9 V, 10.0 V at exactly 20 V and at most 10.86 V at 21.5 V against the AO3401A limit of +-12 V. D5 starts to conduct between 18.8 and 21.2 V on VBUS, so at a 20 V contract it can already carry a few tens of uA (at most 14 uA at exactly 20 V, 29 uA at 21.5 V); it is needed from 23.8 V, where the divider alone would exceed 12 V (171 uA at the 35.5 V clamp of the TVS). What a healthy board shows at bring-up, with the tolerance of the contract voltage: 4.2 to 4.8 V (9 V), 5.6 to 6.4 V (12 V), 6.9 to 8.0 V (15 V), 9.0 to 10.6 V (20 V; the lower values mean D5 is clamping, which is normal). At a 9 V contract the gate now gets nominally 4.4 to 4.5 V, which is the data sheet's 60 mOhm point (4.5 V) instead of its 85 mOhm point (2.5 V): about 0.28 W instead of up to 0.40 W at the 2.17 A peak - both with the 25 C limits; at operating temperature the A3 figure is 0.4 to 0.6 W (N-2). The alternative 49.9k / 100k was rejected: 2.35 V at 7.30 V is below the lowest gate voltage with a guaranteed RDS(on). C7 sets the turn-on: simulated surge into the input capacitors at a 20 V contract 3.3 A with 100 nF (the A2 values gave 7.2 A, 100k / 100k with 10 nF would give 9.6 A; see N-6 for what remains); the voltage on C7 is VGS, at most 11 V. Turn-off takes 6 to 13 ms with the controller driving the pin (about 10 ms at 20 V) and up to 30 ms when the controller is unpowered; the regulator may re-enable a few times while the switch fades out. A2 needed about 1 ms; nothing behind the switch needs a fast turn-off. Lines saved: 150k, 10 nF. The Zener wording, the bring-up ranges and the turn-off times were corrected in review round 1 (R-6) |
| C-4 | **Enable divider of the buck converter:** R11 / R12 = 100k / 20k instead of 470k / 100k. Turn-on 7.08 / 7.39 / 7.69 V (min / typ / max), turn-off 6.49 / 6.79 / 7.08 V; before: 7.02 V and 6.45 V typical | Still 1.58 V above vSafe5V (5.5 V), 0.86 V below the lowest 9 V source voltage (8.55 V) at start-up, off before the converter runs out of input voltage at full load (5.85 to 6.01 V), EN pin at most 3.64 V at 21.5 V. The 0.86 V are the start-up margin, with the regulator unloaded. Under load (added in review round 1, R-6) the lower bound is the lowest valid sink voltage of a 9 V contract, 7.30 V at the socket, minus 0.2 to 0.3 V in Q1 and copper, against a turn-off of up to 7.08 V (7.26 V if the hysteresis is 70 mV; the data sheet gives 100 mV as typical only): no margin at the theoretical peak, 0.34 V or more at 12 W, about 0.75 V in the static corner. On a 9 V contract with a long or thin cable, from about 0.5 ohm loop resistance, a restart loop is therefore possible. It is not damage: once the regulator is off, the unloaded input is above the turn-on threshold again. A2 kept about 0.35 V more. 100k / 20k stays; test 4 of the guide measures the margin: on the 20 W charger (9 V contract) with the module under load, VBUS_SW at C8 / C9 must stay above about 7.5 V. The alternative 100k / 24.9k was rejected (turn-off 5.43 to 5.91 V lies in drop-out); the reviewers' 49.9k / 12k was not taken (R-7). The feedback divider stays 100k / 24.9k, the data sheet's 5 V row: 4.86 / 5.02 / 5.17 V, up to 5.23 V at light load. Line saved: 470k |
| C-5 | **One 1 uF capacitor: Samsung CL10B105KB8NQNC, 50 V X7R 0603 (C5199872)** for C1 and C2 on VBUS and for the regulator bypass pins C3 (VDDD), C5 (VCCD), C11 (buck VCC). It replaces the 1 uF 50 V X5R, the 100 nF 50 V 0603 and the 1 uF 25 V X7R | No rating goes down: X7R everywhere, 50 V everywhere. Under bias it keeps more than both old parts (0.89 uF at 5 V against 0.83 uF, 0.31 uF at 20 V against 0.15 uF). The VBUS bypass in front of the switch rises from 0.76 uF to 1.78 uF at attach (USB asks for 1 to 10 uF). The Basic 50 V X5R part was not used for the regulator pins: 0.66 uF at 5 V and an 85 C class next to the hottest part of the board. Lines saved: two |
| C-6 | **LEDs: three part numbers instead of four.** Red D4 / D9: Hubei KENTO KT-0603R (C2286, JLCPCB Basic) instead of Lite-On LTST-C191KRKT, with R22 = 1k instead of 150 ohm. Green D6 (PWR), D7 (status), D10 (ACT): KT-0603G; the Wurth 150060VS75000 is gone; R15 and R18 = 10k | KT-0603R gives 145-300 mcd at 20 mA, so 1.4 mA (1.0 to 1.9 mA) is enough for about 11 to 27 mcd where the Lite-On part needed 9 mA; at that current its lower rating (25 mA, 40 mW, steeper derating - this reverses A2-13) is loaded with 28 % at 85 C. Status LEDs, estimated from the data sheet curves (intensities corrected in review round 1, R-6): green about 10 to 55 mcd (5 V, 10k; 0.27 mA is below the range of the data sheet curve), blue 23-57 mcd (2.2k), red about 11 to 27 mcd. D10 runs from 3.3 V through 1k like the activity LED of the CM4 IO board: 0.9 mA typical, about 0.1 mA with every limit against it - it lights on every board but its brightness varies about 10 : 1. JLCPCB's footprint of C2286 numbers the pads the other way round (pad 1 = anode); `make_jlc.py` fits by pin name ("terminals swapped by pin name" in the placement report), rotations of D4 and D9 unchanged. Lines saved: 150 ohm, Wurth LED |
| C-7 | **R19 / R21, the gate pull-downs of the status-LED transistors: 2.2k instead of 10k** (no effect on the BOM) | Found while reading the CM4 data sheet: GPIO5 and GPIO6 power up with a pull-up of 33 to 73 kOhm; against 10k that is up to 0.79 V at the gate, where a 2N7002 at its minimum threshold passes 10-20 uA - a faint glow of the status LEDs during boot on some boards. With 2.2k it is 0.21 V; the GPIO drives 1.5 mA when the LED is on |
| C-8 | **Not merged, each for a reason:** R41 in the PDM clock, which is 75 ohm since review round 1 (R-1). It was 33 ohm here, a value sized against the 4.0 V limit of the old microphone: in the fast corner of the simulation it peaks at 3.81 / 3.94 V against the 3.6 V limit of the IM72D128. 100 ohm exceeds the microphone's 13 ns edge limit in the slow corner (13.4 ns at MK2), 150 ohm would give edges of up to 18 ns, and 0 ohm overshoots to 4.0-4.3 V; R16 = 49.9k on the microSD switch (20k would limit at 1.2-1.4 A on a rail rated 600 mA); R14 = 24.9k and R27 = 12.0k (feedback, hub bias); the hub strap resistors (pull-downs must be 47-100k, the port-disable pull-ups 10k); C10 = 220 nF 50 V X7R (the part TI demands at VIN); the 4.7 uF line (bulk for the SD card, the radio and the port switches); the eight 22 uF (only all eight together reach the output capacitance TI's table asks for after DC bias) | A line is worth 1.53 USD; none of these is |
| C-9 | **The hub filter capacitors C28 / C29 stay 100 nF.** A suspected defect (1 uF required on CRFILT / PLLFILT) was checked and is none | USB251xB data sheet DS00001692E, table 3-2: the pins "can have up to a 0.1 uF low-ESR capacitor to VSS, or be left unconnected"; the 1 uF figure comes from Microchip's 2009 evaluation board, whose 2013 revision carries 0.1 uF |
| C-10 | **Basic parts were preferred only where they are free:** the red LED. Not swapped: the 4.7 uF line (the Basic part is rated 16 V instead of 25 V) and the 2N7002 (the Basic part is a 115 mA / +-20 V type) | No fee to save (C-1), so a lower rating buys nothing |
| C-11 | **Via covering stays "Epoxy Filled & Capped"; the vias stay in the exposed pads.** The alternative was worked out to the end - a thermal model of the real copper, JLCPCB's published rules, and a layout variant that was built and passed ERC, DRC and all gates - and then not adopted | Asked for: move the thermal vias out of the exposed pads if that makes the free "Plugged" option safe without hurting thermals; it would save 20.96 USD (form prices of 2026-11-20). JLCPCB plugs a via only if it has no solder mask opening on either side and keeps 0.35 mm from every pad opening; a via in a pad is never plugged, and the plugged surface "may not be completely level". (1) Thermals: a 3-D conduction model of the board (0.1 mm cells, validated against hand calculations) gives for the buck converter U2 +2.9 to +5.5 K at the theoretical peak when its four in-window vias go, where the board has no margin left (N-2); the amplifier U10 loses its only via and runs 13 K hotter at continuous full power (1 K with speech). (2) The hub U4 cannot give up the vias under its package: the exposed pad is its only ground ("must be tied to ground with multiple vias", Microchip), a track through the one free corner would be about 0.5 nH instead of 0.04 nH. The only way to keep them is a solder mask strip across the pad with the vias under it - a ridge of unspecified height under the middle of a QFN-36 and, for the PD controller, a QFN-24; Infineon's layout note warns against covered vias in the soldered pad. A lifted U1 is a dead board, and nobody can rework these boards. (3) The best variant found: U2 with 10 vias in its mask-covered pad ends and 6 outside the body (+2 K), U4 and U1 with 5 and 3 vias under a 1.3 mm mask strip, U10 with one via at a ground lead; 306 vias, placement and CPL unchanged. It stays the documented fallback for a later run of boards whose first samples can be inspected - not for two boards that must work the first time. Filled and capped vias are flat and closed: 21 USD remain cheap insurance (A2-29) |
| C-12 | **R28, the pull-up of the hub's VBUS_DET input: 10k instead of 100k** (no effect on the BOM) | Found in the data sheet check: with 100k and the maximum input leakage of 10 uA the pin sits at 2.21 V against a threshold of 2.0 V - 0.21 V of margin in the worst case; with 10k it is 1.12 V. The pin only tells the hub that the upstream host is present. The same criterion applies to the mute sense input GPIO27 (R24 = 100k to ground) and had not been applied there; review round 1 settled it in software with the internal pull-down of the input: unmuted at most 0.42 V, muted 2.21 V in the worst case (R-3) |

### Found on the way: the existing design against its data sheets (documented, copper not changed)

| # | Finding | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| N-1 | **Port switches U5 / U6:** the nearest input capacitors (C37, C40) are 10.9 and 16.7 mm from the IN pins and the current-limit resistors R37 / R39 have 14 and 20 mm of track; TI asks for "as close as possible" and "as short as possible". The microSD switch U3 is fine (2.4 mm, 3 mm) | Low. The 5 V supply reaches the pins through the plane, the outputs have 100 uF each; the limit accuracy can be slightly worse than the 1.19 to 1.39 A computed. The parts were placed by the automatic placer; moving them would mean a new placement and a new route of the signal layers three review rounds have looked at. Open item for the next layout revision |
| N-2 | **The theoretical peak load cannot be held continuously inside the closed enclosure, and on a 9 V contract the load switch Q1 is the limiting part** (figures corrected in review round 1, R-5). Regulator: TI's efficiency curves give 1.34 W (9 V in) to 1.63 W (20 V in) at 3.45 A, not the 1.1 W the A2 documents assumed. These are losses at a junction of about 70 C; at the junction temperature the model predicts they are about 1.6 to 1.9 W (TI Fig. 9-4). The conduction model gives 37.5 K/W from the regulator's pad to ambient plus about 29 K from the neighbours: at 50 C around the board a junction of about 150 to 165 C, against the 125 C rating and the 150 C absolute maximum. At a sustained theoretical peak the regulator is therefore expected to cycle through its thermal shutdown (165 C off, 148 C on). Q1 (AO3401A, SOT-23 beside the regulator) has no thermal protection. The same model, run again on the A3 board, gives 114 K/W from its drain pad to ambient (109 to 124 K/W for 15 to 5 W/m2K); the data sheet adds 63 typ / 80 max K/W junction to lead, the neighbours 24 K at 12 W and 44 K at the peak. On a 9 V contract Q1 dissipates 0.14 to 0.22 W at 12 W and 0.4 to 0.6 W at the theoretical peak (0.28 W only with the 25 C limit of RDS(on)): junction about 100 to 120 C at 12 W, 115 to 150 C at 13.9 W (both USB ports at 500 mA, a busy module, no sine tone) and above the 150 C absolute maximum at the theoretical peak (about 150 to 165 C for a typical part; a part at the RDS(on) limit reaches 150 C at about 14 to 15 W). At the theoretical peak on a 12 / 15 / 20 V contract it is 128 to 144 / 116 to 124 / 110 to 113 C | The peak is the sum of everything at its maximum at once (both USB ports at 500 mA, a 3.2 W sine in the speaker, the module at 7 W). At the realistic maximum of 12 W (2.4 A, regulator loss about 0.7 to 1.0 W) the same model gives about 100 to 113 C for the regulator, at typical load about 76 C. All of this is a model with an assumed cooling coefficient (10 W/m2K; at the 1.1 W loss it was first run with, 114 to 151 C for 15 to 5), not a measurement: measure the regulator, the inductor and Q1 on the first board, Q1 on a 9 V contract at about 12 W, and do not hold the full peak on 9 V. What follows for the charger: on a 9 V contract keep the 5 V rail at or below about 12 W. For more, the contract must be 12 V or higher: a charger with a fixed 12 V, 15 V or 20 V profile of at least 2 A - in practice 30 W or more, or a 27 W unit with 12 V / 2.25 A. A charger whose highest profile of 2 A or more is 9 V - that includes 27 W units without a 12 V offer - is for loads up to about 12 W only. The 30 W charger of the shopping list negotiates 15 V. No value or copper was changed; the options the reviewers offered are in R-7 |
| N-3 | **Power budget figures corrected:** power stage about 89.7 % (9 V) and 88.4 % (20 V) efficient at the peak, not 92 %; hub 95 mA typical / 110 mA maximum with two ports (was 70 / 150 mA); amplifier about 86 % at 3.2 W into 4 ohm (was 90 %); output voltage up to 5.23 V at light load. The theoretical peak at the connector is about 19.6 W (was 19.2 W), 9 % above an 18 W contract | README.md and the guides carry the corrected numbers. The charger advice was corrected in review round 1 (N-2, R-5): for loads above about 12 W the charger must offer a fixed 12 V, 15 V or 20 V profile of at least 2 A, in practice 30 W or more. "27 W or larger" alone was not enough, because such a charger may offer 2 A or more only at 9 V |
| N-4 | **Inductor current reserve:** at 3.45 A the worst-case inductor peak is 4.51 A against a minimum current limit of 4.8 A; at the regulator's rated 4 A it would be 5.06 A | The 3.45 A budget is covered with 0.29 A reserve; the stage is not a guaranteed 4 A supply at 20 V input. Added in review round 1 (R-6): the budget holds only while both USB-A ports together stay at about 1 A (2 x 500 mA). The 1.3 A limit per port (1.19 to 1.39 A) is short-circuit protection, not an allowance: with both ports at their limit the 5 V rail is asked for 4.1 to 4.5 A without the amplifier and 4.8 to 5.2 A with it, the regulator runs in current limit and the module restarts. A larger charger does not cure that |
| N-5 | **U4 has two vias in its exposed pad**, which is its only ground connection; Microchip asks for "multiple vias" | Two is the minimum reading of "multiple"; the computed ground inductance is about 0.04 nH. More vias would be better and are noted for the next layout revision |
| N-6 | **Switch-on surge of the load switch** (simulation with the typical transfer curve of the AO3401A): 7.2 A for about 40 us at a 20 V contract with the A2 values, above the contract current and slightly above the USB PD limit for the rate of change (150 mA/us) | Reduced by C-3, not removed (wording corrected in review round 1, R-6). With C7 = 100 nF the rate of change is inside the USB PD limit (0.03 A/us) and the peak falls from about 7 to 8 A to about 3 A (3.3 A in the authors' simulation; 2.7 A typical and 3.7 A in the corner in the reviewer's). That is still above the requested 2 A for about 50 us at a 20 V contract (about 2 A at 15 V, below 1 A at 9 V). Accepted: the charger's output capacitor serves a pulse of that length. The scope measurement of open item 9 stays |

### Final stock and price check (live, JLCPCB assembly library and LCSC, 2026-11-20 22:23 to 22:46 UTC)

The figures below are those of the first reading, which led to the part changes. The sourcing file, the BOM and the
order cost were read again after the changes. That repetition ended at 00:07 UTC on 2026-11-21; the documents call the
two together the read of 2026-11-20. All 44 lines and two of the three replaced parts (the Wurth connector and the IM69D130V01; the tray part of the
hub, C220679, was last read on 2026-11-20) were read once more on 2026-11-21 at 09:57
UTC in review round 1, after R41 had changed (`hardware/gen/sourcing_jlc.json`, field `checked`); every price and
total of the current-state sections comes from this second read. No line is below twice the need; the thin lines are
unchanged (ADAU7002ACBZ-R7 63, RF-BM-2652P2 126). JLCPCB's USD prices had moved up by about 0.24 % on almost every
line since the reading before (exchange rate): RF-BM-2652P2 is 8.6843 USD at 1 piece and 7.4785 at 10, ADAU7002ACBZ-R7
2.4754 / 2.0991, IM72D128 3.1937 / 2.7094; MM3Z10VT1G fell to 0.0382 USD (stock 21,092). Parts per board: 39.84 USD at
single-piece prices, 29.30 USD at 100-piece prices; order of two boards 79.68 USD at the needed quantities, 83.22 USD
with minimum quantities, 84.99 USD with minimum and loss quantities. Section 10 carries this reading. The prices of
JLCPCB's order form (PCB, assembly fees) were read on 2026-11-20, the shipping prices on 2026-11-20 and on
2026-11-21 between 00:30 and 01:00 UTC; none of them was read again with the parts at 09:57 UTC.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| F-1 | **U4 is ordered as USB2514BT-I/M2 (C510949)** instead of USB2514B-I/M2 (C220679) | 2 pieces left of the tray part (1,182 on 2026-11-12), 2 needed - below twice the need. The "T" part is the same device on tape and reel (data sheet p. 55: the identifier "is not printed on the device package"), 6,206 in stock, same pads, same pin-1 corner, CPL angle unchanged |
| F-2 | **J6 is ordered as Jing Extension 907-112A1022D10200 (C29780026)**, the Wurth 61400826021 becomes the alternate | The Wurth part fell from 116 to 18 pieces in eight days and would very likely be gone on the day of the order; the Jing part has 2,434 and costs 0.36 instead of 2.75 USD. Checked against both drawings and the board: pins match, the front shell stakes sit 0.12 mm forward, shell 0.26 mm taller (2.4 mm left to the base), flange 14.6 x 15.7 mm in the 15.6 mm window, 1.5 A and 1500 cycles as before. The stakes fit the Wurth land pattern at nominal dimensions: they are 0.0 to 0.1 mm clear in the smallest drilled hole, depending on how the part centres itself. That is less than the part tolerance (+-0.25 mm) and the hole position tolerance (+-0.075 mm), so the stakes, which are spring legs, may need light pressure (corrected in review round 1, R-6; this entry had given 0.08 mm as a margin). Accepted: its drawing states no temperature range, four of its dimensions are scaled from the drawing, not dimensioned. JLCPCB has a footprint record for it, so all 136 placements are now compared with JLCPCB's own footprints (the Wurth part had none). That record confirms position, rotation and pin 1 of J6, and the fit of the real part rests on the Jing drawing alone (R-6). This entry had also said that the record carries the Wurth land pattern, which is not exact: its pin rows are 2.71 mm apart against 2.62 mm on the board, 0.045 mm per row at the CPL point (OP-5, R-23). The USB-A window of the enclosure is 0.3 mm longer for its taller flange (15.6 x 17.3 mm) |
| F-3 | **The microphones are ordered as Infineon IM72D128V01XTMA1 (C5119248)**, the IM69D130V01 becomes the alternate; C43 / C44 at their supply pins become 1 uF 25 V X5R 0402 (CL05A105KA5NQNC, C52923, Basic) | The IM69D130V01 fell from 392 to 43 pieces in eight days and Infineon lists it as not for new designs; the IM72D128 (623 pieces) has the same land pattern (package PG-LLGA-5-3 against PG-LLGA-5-1), pin-out, timing and -36 dBFS sensitivity and the same 2.9 to 3.3 MHz clock window (the other windows are narrower: the sound card must stay at 48 kHz, which the googlevoicehat codec driver enforces), 72 instead of 69 dB SNR, overload point 128 instead of 130 dBSPL, absolute maximum 3.6 V instead of 4.0 V on the 3.3 V rail (at most 3.38 V). Two consequences: its data sheet asks for 1 uF at VDD "to ensure best SNR performance" (one new BOM line), and the microphone with SELECT = VDD (MK2, right channel) puts a constant level of typically -40 dBFS into its data stream, which the ADAU7002 passes on - see W-1. The clock input limit (VDD + 0.3 V) is the same as before, but the absolute maximum on the clock pin fell from 4.0 V to 3.6 V with the part, and the new data sheet gives no hysteresis figure. This entry had quoted only the nominal result of the clock simulation with the old R41 = 33 ohm (3.52 V at a 3.30 V rail). Review round 1 showed that the same simulation gives 3.81 / 3.94 V at MK1 / MK2 in its fast corner; R41 is 75 ohm since then: 3.41 V nominal and 3.53 / 3.54 V in the fast corner, with assumed driver data (R-1), to be measured on the first board (open item 12). The cheaper IM73D122 (875 pieces) was not taken: it is 10 dB more sensitive and every gain default and gain advice of the software would have to move |
| F-4 | **Two thin lines remain and have no alternate:** ADAU7002ACBZ-R7 (63 pieces, was 104; price 2.47 USD, was 3.91) and RF-BM-2652P2 (126 pieces; price 8.66 USD, was 6.63). Reserve them first as the order guide says | No other PDM-to-I2S converter is stocked. The only other stocked CC2652P module with this footprint, RF-BM-2652P2I (C5440077, 165 pieces on 2026-11-21), has a socket for an external antenna in place of the printed antenna and is not an alternate for the board as it is (order guide, section 2.4; corrected in review round 2, R-47). The spacers 9774015151R are back at 2,091 pieces |
| F-5 | **The board is revision A3** (title block, silkscreen "VoxHub CM4  rev A3") | Values, part numbers and three land patterns (K-6) differ from A2; a board must say which BOM belongs to it |

### Software

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| W-1 | **The capture path of the VoxHub Assistant add-on removes a constant level** (`DcBlocker` in `audio.py`: one-pole high-pass, 12.7 Hz corner at 16 kHz, -0.05 dB at 100 Hz) and applies the gain after it, before the single rounding to 16 bit; six tests (two came with the filter, four in review round 1, R-4); a seventh, for the default gain of 32, came with R-27 | With the IM72D128 the mono mix carries a constant of about 166 LSB (662 after gain 4, about 5300 after the default gain of 32 that R-27 set), which the energy-based end-pointer of this add-on cannot tell from speech. In the pipeline, whose noise tracker follows the level, version 0.1.0 then finds no request ("No speech heard", a time-out); only with an untracked noise floor does it find no end of a request (wording corrected in review round 1, R-4). The filter is harmless for the IM69D130. The two-device path (Linux Voice Assistant) was not changed and passes the level on. In simulations with the test recordings of its wake word library, voice activity detection gave the same result with and without the level, and so did the wake word from 60 dBSPL at the microphone; for quieter speech (45 to 55 dBSPL) the wake word was found 4 to 14 percentage points less often with the level. This entry had said that neither depends on the level; the second part of review round 1 corrected that (SW-1, R-26). Not tested on hardware |

### Local editing in KiCad 10 (milestone 3)

The owner will edit the project by hand in KiCad 10 on Windows. Until this milestone every generating build step
rewrote the project files from `hardware/gen` without looking at them, and the fabrication files could only be made
with Docker. The package below was built and tested with KiCad 10.0.8 on Linux (the project image, and the GUI on
the virtual display Xvfb, operated by a script, not by a person). Final state: `SKIP_ROUTE=1 hardware/build.sh all`
and then `hardware/build.sh outputs` end with exit code 0, ERC 0 errors / 0 warnings, DRC 0 violations /
0 unconnected / 0 footprint errors, 4 warnings in the waived-check report, all gates passed; the package by itself
left the board geometrically identical (pcbnew + shapely comparison, rendered Gerbers without difference on all
layers). Not tested: anything on Windows (KiCad's editors, saves with
CR LF, the jobset, WSL2, Git Bash), `KICAD_NATIVE=1`, and a fresh autoroute without `SKIP_ROUTE=1`. The set-up the
build is meant for on Windows is WSL2 with Docker; for Git Bash only the `docker run` call of `build.sh` is protected
against path conversion (`MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`). The independent test of this package is in the second part of review
round 1 below: it found two high, one medium and eight low problems (T-01 to T-11), all of which were fixed or
decided (R-8 to R-18), and a second independent test of the fixes found no high or medium problem (R-30 to R-34).
The rows K-11 to K-14 were corrected in place where a fix changed what they describe. `KICAD_NATIVE=1` was run in
that round for the steps `sch`, `pcb` and `route`, inside the KiCad container only. How to use the package is
described in `hardware/README.md`.

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| K-11 | **A guard keeps `build.sh` from overwriting manual edits.** `hardware/gen/guard.py` keeps the SHA-256 sums of the generated project files in `hardware/gen/generated.sha256` (42 lines: the six schematic sheets, `voxhub.kicad_sym`, the 33 footprints of `voxhub.pretty`, `voxhub.kicad_pcb`, `voxhub.kicad_pro`). Before a generating step (`sch`, `pcb`, `route`, `all`) writes anything, it compares the files on disk with that record. If one was changed, added or deleted - saved from KiCad or edited by hand - the step stops, names the files and writes nothing. `OVERWRITE=1 hardware/build.sh <step>` discards the edits and regenerates; `hardware/build.sh outputs` is never blocked. Since the second part of review round 1 the guard also stops when the record is missing or lacks a line, when the last run of the step did not finish and when KiCad has the project open, and it checks again before every generator command of a step (R-8 to R-18) | One `build.sh all` after an afternoon in the schematic editor would have overwritten the work without a warning. CR LF counts as LF, so a Windows checkout or a save by KiCad on Windows is not a change (KiCad 10 writes these files in text mode: read from its source, not observed on Windows). Tested with the real `build.sh`: a text added to a sheet or to the board, a changed project file, an added and a deleted footprint each stop the step with exit code 1 and leave every file as it was; `route` stops before the autorouter starts; all 42 files converted to CR LF pass, and a real edit in such a file is still reported. As built in this milestone, a step recorded the files it had written at its end, as they were on disk, also when the step had failed; a save made while the step ran therefore passed as generated (T-02). Now the sum of a file is recorded directly after the generator command that wrote it, nothing is recorded after a failed generator command, and the project file is recorded only when it equals `gen/project_settings.json` (R-9). Limits, by design: KiCad stores the last used export paths in `voxhub.kicad_pro`, which counts as a change of the project file; a build step that was stopped or killed half way, or one of whose generator commands failed, needs `OVERWRITE=1` on the next run, and since R-9 the next run says so (`unfinished`); Ctrl-C, which did not stop a step when this row was written, now ends the step and its container (R-12); a board or project file that is saved while a board-writing generator command runs is lost without a message, which is why KiCad has to be closed before a generating step (R-30); the record has to be committed together with the regenerated files, because the board file is not byte-reproducible. One limit named here in milestone 3 was a fault and is gone: without the record nothing was protected, and a step rewrote only its own lines, so that an edit of the board passed after `build.sh sch` (T-01); a missing or incomplete record now stops the generating steps with `not recorded` (R-8). Not guarded, because KiCad cannot edit them: the netlist model, the placement file and the autorouter session |
| K-12 | **`hardware/build.sh outputs` makes the fabrication files from the project as it is on disk and leaves `voxhub.kicad_pro` byte for byte as it found it** | This is the build step that keeps manual edits: the DRC with schematic parity gates it as before, then Gerbers, drill files, position file, DXF, layer PDF, STEP, renders, the gate reports and the JLCPCB BOM / CPL are made from the edited board. The JLCPCB BOM takes the part of a footprint from its field `MPN` alone, through `gen/sourcing_jlc.json`, and does not read the value: a value changed in KiCad without that field leaves the old part in `bom_jlcpcb.csv` with exit code 0 (review round 2, TL-1: R41 changed from 75 to 100 in sheet and board by a script, the BOM line is still `0402WGF750JTCE`, C25133), so the field has to be changed with the value. A part to be fitted that has no field `MPN`, as every symbol newly placed from `voxhub.kicad_sym`, was left out of BOM and CPL without a message (R41 without the field: exit code 0, 43 instead of 44 BOM lines, 135 instead of 136 placements); since review round 2 `gen/make_jlc.py` reports it as `PROBLEM: <reference>: no field MPN, ...` and the step ends with exit code 1. Until now its waived-check DRC run rewrote the project file from `gen/project_settings.json`. In this milestone that run worked on a variant of the file on disk (the two waived checks as warnings) and put the original back, also when the run failed. Since the second part of review round 1 it works on a copy of the whole project under `gen/work/waived/`, which is deleted afterwards, and `outputs` writes no project file at all (T-02, T-04; R-9, R-11). Tested with a project file and a sheet edited by hand: exit code 0, project file identical before and after, the record of K-11 untouched, 4 warnings in the waived-check report. Not refreshed by `outputs`: `outputs/erc.rpt` and the schematic PDF (only `build.sh sch` writes them: run the ERC in KiCad or use the jobset, K-13), and `outputs/bom.csv`, `bom_system.csv` and `order_cost.*`, which come from the code model `gen/model_netlist.json`, not from an edited schematic. Two more files are not refreshed and were missing from this list (T-07, R-14): `outputs/mechanical_positions.csv`, which the placement in `pcb` / `route` writes, and `outputs/usb_impedance.txt`, which no build step writes. Also changed: `build.sh sch` writes the project file as well, so that the ERC of the first build after a settings change no longer runs with the previous severities. Not covered in this milestone: a hard kill during the roughly 15 s of the waived-check run left the variant in the place of the project file, with the original in a spare copy under `gen/work/`. That case no longer exists: after such a kill the project file is untouched, the copy under `gen/work/waived/` stays, and the next `outputs` run deletes it (R-11) |
| K-13 | **`hardware/voxhub.kicad_jobset` makes reports and fabrication files from the KiCad 10 project manager, without Docker** (open the jobset in the project tree, *Generate All Destinations*). Nine jobs, two destinations: ERC and DRC reports, schematic PDF, Gerbers, drill files with maps, position file and STEP into `outputs/`, and the 13 Gerber and drill files of the order into `../fab/jlcpcb/gerbers.zip` | The owner has KiCad on Windows and perhaps no working Docker there. The options are those of `build.sh outputs`: on the same board the files are identical apart from their date lines (compared after `kicad-cli jobset run` and after a run from the KiCad 10.0.8 project manager, both on Linux; the project manager ran on the virtual display under a script). Limits that cannot be solved inside the file: (1) it does not stop on a violation - a failed ERC or DRC job only marks the first destination red (kicad-cli: exit code 6), all files and the zip are written all the same, so use them only when the first destination is not red; only `build.sh outputs` refuses to plot a board with violations; (2) run with `kicad-cli jobset run`, KiCad 10.0.8 skips the schematic parity part of the DRC job and still reports success, while from the project manager parity runs (tested with a deliberate mismatch: 2 footprint errors in the GUI, 0 from kicad-cli); (3) the position file is named `voxhub-all-pos.csv`, with the content of `outputs/voxhub_pos.csv`; (4) BOM, JLCPCB BOM / CPL, DXF, layer PDF, renders, the waived-check report and the gate reports come from `build.sh outputs` only; (5) the jobset is a static file: when a plot or drill option of `build.sh` changes, it has to be changed in the jobset by hand. Found in the second part of review round 1: after a build through Docker the board, the footprints and `outputs/gerbers` belonged to root, and a jobset run by a normal user then left the old Gerbers in place while kicad-cli still reported every job as succeeded (T-03). `build.sh` now gives the files back to the owner of `hardware/` at the end of every container call (R-10). `outputs/voxhub-all-pos.csv` is in `.gitignore`; the tracked `outputs/voxhub_pos.csv` and `outputs/voxhub_gerbers.zip` are not refreshed by a jobset run (R-14) |
| K-14 | **Every ERC and DRC check is an error except four ignored keys, and the board file stores the plot settings of `build.sh`.** 30 keys went to error (9 DRC, 21 ERC). Ignored: `lib_footprint_mismatch` and `footprint_type_mismatch`, the two waived library comparisons, which the waived-check run still shows (`outputs/drc_waived_checks_enabled.rpt`: 4 warnings - U2 type; J4, J6, C13 library comparison); `footprint_filters_mismatch` (DRC) and `footprint_filter` (ERC), because the footprint filters of 6 of the 151 symbols cannot match their footprint: H5-H8 (filter `MountingHole*Pad*` against the name of the Wurth spacer footprint) and J5, J7 (filter `Connector*:*_1x??_*`, which names a library, against a link of the form `voxhub:<name>`). This row had given the reason for every symbol, which was too general. Plot settings in the board: output directory `outputs/gerbers/`, the 11 layers, Protel extensions, solder mask subtracted from silkscreen, no X2, no netlist attributes | A manual edit must not get through with a warning that nobody reads: a warning can no longer pass unnoticed (this goes beyond K-5). No other check fired on the board, so no key had to stay a warning. With the stored settings *File > Plot* writes the same Gerbers as `build.sh` (KiCad 10.0.8 GUI on Linux, on the virtual display under a script: identical apart from the date lines; using the dialog changes neither the board nor the project file) - this replaces the warning of K-10. Not possible for the drill files: KiCad keeps the settings of *Generate Drill Files* per user, not in the project; with its own defaults that dialog writes the four plated slots of J1 as route commands and writes no drill maps. Take the drill files from the jobset or from `build.sh outputs`. Found on the way: the waived-check report is not gated. A bug made and fixed in this work (the solder mask layers were dropped from every pad with a hole) passed the strict DRC and every gate and showed only as 18 instead of 4 warnings in that report |
| K-15 | **Stand-in 3D model for the two CM4 sockets J2 / J3:** `hardware/3dmodels/Connector_Hirose_DF40.3dshapes/Hirose_DF40C-100DS-0.4V_2x50_P0.4mm.step`, written by `gen/make_3dmodels.py` | The 3D viewer and the STEP export reported a missing model for J2 and J3. Envelope from the Hirose DF40 catalogue (edition 2013.11, the mirror named in section 9): 22.6 x 2.88 x 1.45 mm, 3.38 mm over the contact tails; it agrees with the fab outline of the footprint and with the 1.5 mm stacking height of the enclosure model. Simplified on purpose: the inside is a plain trough, open at both ends, that clears the plug block of the existing CM4 model (no common volume, smallest distance 0.05 mm), and each side has one strip instead of 50 contacts. The STEP export of the board now runs without a "Could not add 3D model" line. Not done: a faithful socket with closed ends, which would need the CM4 model regenerated with the real header size |

### Review round 1 (2026-11-21): four independent reviewers on the changed areas

Each reviewer got one area that revision A3 had changed - the PD sink and the load switch; the buck enable divider,
the LEDs and the pull resistors; the three replaced parts; the software -, the repository and the research notes.
Each fetched the manufacturers' documents again and recomputed or simulated the values with its own scripts. Result:
**no high finding**, 4 medium and 22 low. The two medium findings on the hardware (the PDM clock, R-1, and the
temperature of Q1, R-5) were each confirmed by a second, independent agent whose task was to refute them. The two
medium findings of the software reviewer (SW-1, SW-2: what the documents say about the wake word in the two-device
path and about the microphone gain the end-pointer needs) went to second checkers as well. Their results came after
this table was written and are in the second part of this round below: SW-1 was partly confirmed (R-26), SW-2 was
confirmed (R-27). Nothing was measured on hardware: every figure
below is a calculation, a simulation or a model result. The identifiers in brackets are the reviewers' own (PD, BS,
RP, SW).

| # | Finding (severity) | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| R-1 | **PDM clock at the new microphones (medium, RP-1; confirmed by a second agent with a model of its own).** The microphone change of F-3 lowered the absolute maximum on any pin from 4.0 V (IM69D130) to 3.6 V (IM72D128), and the new data sheet gives no hysteresis figure. R41 = 33 ohm had been sized against 4.0 V and was not evaluated again; the documents quoted only the nominal 3.52 V of the simulation (star at R41 with branches of 43.3 mm to MK1 and 94.1 mm to MK2; driver and input data are assumptions). The same model gives 3.61 V at the 3.3825 V top of the module's rail tolerance and, in its fast corner, 3.81 / 3.94 V at MK1 / MK2 (3.90 / 4.03 V at the top of the rail), with a fall-back to 1.74 V after crossing VIH | **R41 = 75 ohm** (UNI-ROYAL 0402WGF750JTCE, C25133, JLCPCB "Preferred Extended", 0.0012 USD, 994,323 in stock on 2026-11-21). The 33 ohm line (C25105, Basic) is gone; the BOM stays at 44 assembled lines and 136 placements, the resistor values at 9, and the library types are now 12 Basic / 2 Preferred Extended (49.9k and 75 ohm) / 30 Extended (were 13 / 1 / 30). Three independent simulations agree: the authors' stored model run again, the reviewer's and the verifier's. Authors' model, without track loss: 3.41 V nominal (3.50 V at the top of the rail); fast corner 3.53 / 3.54 V at 3.30 V and 3.62 / 3.63 V at 3.3825 V (the verifier's model with track loss: 3.55 V); no fall-back below VIH in its three corners (the verifier's sweep of 135 assumption sets shows one in 23 sets without and 16 with track loss, to 1.83 / 1.88 V at the lowest, 14 of the 16 with the 0.5 ns driver edge); 10-90 % edge at most 12.0 ns in the slow corner (12.1 ns in the verifier's model with track loss) against the microphone's 13 ns limit. Not taken: 47 / 56 ohm (still 3.66 to 3.72 / 3.62 to 3.64 V in the fast corner) and 100 ohm (12.7 / 13.4 ns in the slow corner, over the limit); 68 ohm (C25131) and 82 ohm (C25136) are equivalent within the accuracy of the model. Not removed by 75 ohm: with a 0.5 ns driver edge the rising edge at MK1 is not monotonic (a reversal of about 0.3 V inside the threshold band; about 0.5 to 0.6 V with 33 to 47 ohm in the reviewers' models). The clock cannot be repaired on assembled boards; open item 12 says what to measure |
| R-2 | **Ground ring of the microphone land pattern too close to its sound hole (found by the order-guide check, not by the DRC).** The ring of MK1 / MK2 ran from 0.58 to 1.04 mm radius and started 0.18 mm from the edge of the 0.8 mm non-plated hole; JLCPCB asks for 0.2 mm between copper and a hole without plating. KiCad's DRC had not reported it: it does not test this ring pad against the hole of its own footprint (the reason was not investigated) | `hardware/gen/make_footprints.py` (`LAND_FIXES`, which now patches three stock footprints: spacer ring, USB-C contacts, microphone ring; K-6) writes the ring from 0.61 to 1.04 mm radius: 0.43 mm wide instead of 0.46 mm, 0.21 mm from the edge of the hole. No copper of the board is now closer than 0.2 mm to a non-plated hole, and the earlier advice to accept a cut-back of 0.02 mm by JLCPCB at the two microphone holes is obsolete. The six paste segments of the ring (0.60 to 1.00 mm radius) were not changed at this point. The second part of this round moved their inner edge to 0.63 mm, because they now began inside the inner edge of the ring, in a band of at most 0.016 mm on solder mask (OP-1, R-19). Board comparison with the commit before: 1384 tracks, 286 vias, zones, filled copper, 330 drill hits (4 slots) and outline identical; only the pads MK1.5 / MK2.5 (copper and mask) and the value of R41 differ. ERC 0 errors / 0 warnings; DRC 0 violations / 0 unconnected / 0 footprint errors; waived-check report 4 warnings (U2 type; J4, J6, C13 library comparison) |
| R-3 | **Mute sense input GPIO27 held low only by R24 = 100k (low, BS-4).** The criterion that made C-12 change R28 - the module's maximum input leakage of 10 uA across 100k - applies to this input as well: unmuted, it gives 1.01 V, above the 0.8 V low threshold. Unchanged since A2; R22 = 1k does not matter here. A wrong reading would make the software show "muted" while the microphones are live | Software only: the internal pull-down of the input is switched on, `gpio_pull=down` in `os/config/config-voxhub.txt` (was off) and `brcm,pull = <1>` in `os/overlay/voxhub-overlay.dts`. With the internal 33 / 47 / 73 kOhm the level is at most 0.42 V unmuted, about 2.5 V typical and 2.21 V in the worst case muted, against the 2.0 V high threshold (without the pull-down: 3.0 V muted). The hardware mute and the red LED D9 do not depend on this input. No hardware change |
| R-4 | **Capture filter of the VoxHub Assistant add-on (three low findings, SW-3 to SW-5).** `DcBlocker` was only valid for short buffers: one call with more than about 8.7 s of audio overflowed (the add-on's own reader, which always passes 1280 samples, cannot reach that). The two tests of W-1 covered the class but not its use: the suite still passed with the filter taken out of the capture path. The filter rounded to 16 bit before the gain, so the signal was quantised twice. And W-1 described the failure of version 0.1.0 as the unit test shows it, not as the pipeline does | Version 0.1.1 (not yet released): the filter processes long buffers in blocks of at most 4096 samples, rejects a pole outside 0..1 and applies the microphone gain before the single rounding to 16 bit. Four new tests: long buffers and other poles against a sample-by-sample recursion; gain before rounding; the real `Microphone` reader removes a constant level before the gain, also after a restart; the pipeline with its noise tracker finds a request on top of a constant level and times out without the filter. That request is a 200 Hz tone of 354 counts rms before the gain, at gain 4, on a constant level of 166 counts. This row had called the request "quiet", which it is not: 354 counts is about the level of a 94 dBSPL tone at these microphones (SW-2, R-27). pytest at that point: 143 tests (92 VoxHub Assistant, 51 VoxHub Satellite), four more than before; ruff clean. Since R-27 there are 144 tests (93 + 51). W-1 now says what happens in the pipeline: version 0.1.0 answers "No speech heard" (a time-out), and only with an untracked noise floor does it find no end of a request |
| R-5 | **Temperature of the load switch Q1 on a 9 V contract (medium, BS-1; confirmed and corrected by a second agent) and of the regulator at the peak (low, BS-2).** N-2 gave only watts for Q1 ("about 0.3 to 0.4 W"), the section 9 row called its thermal resistance on this board undetermined, and README and shopping list still named a charger with 3 A at 9 V as good for the heavy-load or permanent case - while the project's own thermal model puts Q1, which has no thermal protection, at or above its 150 C absolute maximum at the theoretical peak on a 9 V contract. The regulator figure of 135 to 145 C at the peak was about 15 to 20 K optimistic: the 1.34 / 1.63 W are losses at a junction of about 70 C | Text only. No value or copper change is needed for the order, because the recommended 30 W charger negotiates 15 V. N-2, sections 2 and 9 and open item 9 carry the corrected figures. Q1: conduction model run again on the A3 board, 114 K/W from the drain pad to ambient (109 to 124 K/W for 15 to 5 W/m2K) plus 63 typ / 80 max K/W junction to lead; on 9 V 0.14 to 0.22 W at 12 W and 0.4 to 0.6 W at the theoretical peak; junction about 100 to 120 C at 12 W, 115 to 150 C at 13.9 W, above 150 C at the theoretical peak (a part at the RDS(on) limit reaches 150 C at about 14 to 15 W, at about 13 W with the model's poorest cooling); 128 to 144 / 116 to 124 / 110 to 113 C at the theoretical peak on 12 / 15 / 20 V. Regulator: about 1.6 to 1.9 W at the junction temperature the model predicts (TI Fig. 9-4) and then about 150 to 165 C, i.e. cycling through thermal shutdown (165 C off, 148 C on) at a sustained theoretical peak; about 100 to 113 C at 12 W; the 150 C absolute maximum is named beside the 125 C rating. Charger advice: for loads above about 12 W a fixed 12 V, 15 V or 20 V profile of at least 2 A, in practice 30 W or more, or a 27 W unit with 12 V / 2.25 A; a charger whose highest profile of 2 A or more is 9 V is for loads up to about 12 W only. The thermal test of the guide is to measure Q1 on a 9 V contract (the 20 W charger of the test kit) at about 12 W and not to hold the full peak on 9 V. All figures are model results for 50 C around the board with an assumed cooling coefficient |
| R-6 | **Wording and margins: 17 low findings, no value changed** (PD-1 to PD-7, BS-3, BS-5, BS-6, RP-2 to RP-7, SW-6). Statements that were narrower or more favourable than the calculation behind them, one justification with the wrong rating, one missing record, wrong side statements | Corrected where they stood. (1) The switch-on surge is reduced, not removed: about 3 A, still above the requested 2 A for about 50 us at a 20 V contract (N-6; PD-1). (2) D5 starts to conduct between 18.8 and 21.2 V and is needed from 23.8 V; expected VGS at bring-up 4.2-4.8 V (9 V), 5.6-6.4 V (12 V), 6.9-8.0 V (15 V), 9.0-10.6 V (20 V) (C-3, open item 9; PD-2). (3) Turn-off of Q1 6 to 13 ms with the controller driving the pin, up to 30 ms when it is unpowered; open item 9 asks for a scope record of VBUS_SW and +5V when the controller opens Q1 (C-3; PD-3). (4) The surge is judged by the safe operating area, not by the pulsed drain current: about 26 to 38 W for 0.1 ms against about 105 W allowed for 100 us (section 9; PD-4). (5) The VBUS_MIN divider 12k / 2.2k had no stored independent check; the reviewer's is the record: 511 mV (503 to 520 mV) in the 249 to 786 mV window, margins 254 / 266 mV (C-2; PD-5). (6) VDDD supplies 1.26 mA continuously and about 2.7 mA while FAULT is high; for a next revision feed D4 from VBUS through a FET (C-2; PD-6). (7) Turn-off margin of the regulator under load on a 9 V contract: none at the theoretical peak, 0.34 V or more at 12 W, about 0.75 V in the static corner; 100k / 20k is kept and test 4 gets a measurement (C-4; PD-7, BS-3). (8) LED intensities: green about 10 to 55 mcd, red about 11 to 27 mcd; the sentence that red is the weakest of the three is dropped from the guide (C-6; BS-5). (9) Both USB-A ports together about 1 A in the power budget; the 1.3 A per port is short-circuit protection (N-4; BS-6). (10) J6: stake clearance 0.0 to 0.1 mm at nominal dimensions; front face about 2.5 to 2.85 mm beyond the PCB edge, stakes 1.9 to 2.4 mm beyond the bare side of the board; JLCPCB's footprint record confirms position, rotation and pin 1 only (F-2, section 9; RP-2 to RP-4); its pin rows are 2.71 mm apart against 2.62 mm on the board, 0.045 mm per row (OP-5, R-23). (11) Microphones: the same 2.9 to 3.3 MHz clock window, not "the same clock ranges" (F-3; RP-5). (12) Sourcing notes in `hardware/outputs/bom.csv`: the alternates for C43 / C44 are "any 0402 1 uF X5R or X7R rated 25 V or more" and no DigiKey part is named any more (Samsung data: the 25 V part keeps 0.73 uF at 3.3 V, the 10 V and 16 V parts 0.45 to 0.47 uF); the commercial-grade USB2514B/M2 is rated 0 to +85 C; the stock of the old microphone on 2026-11-12 was 392 pieces, which the order guide had wrong (RP-6, RP-7). (13) Test 9 of the guide after the VoxHub Satellite add-on has run on the hub: use `--volume=164619` as in test S9, or set the source volume back to 100 % first (SW-6) |
| R-7 | **Options the reviewers offered and that were not taken** | (1) VBUS_MIN strapped to 12 V (R1 / R2 = 2.2k / 1k, both values are in the BOM): it removes 9 V contracts, but Q1 would still reach 128 to 144 C at the theoretical peak, chargers of 18 to 25 W and 27 W chargers without a 12 V offer would be rejected, the 20 W test charger would be pointless, and a strap value may not change without a new verification. (2) Enable divider 49.9k / 12k instead of 100k / 20k (off at 5.58 / 5.83 / 6.08 V): it gives the A2 margin back, but the turn-off then lies inside the worst-case drop-out band (5.85 to 6.01 V at 3.45 A). (3) A VBUS_SW copper area of about 4.5 x 4.4 mm at the drain of Q1: 76 instead of 114 K/W in the model, about 10 to 20 K less at 13.9 W, but the theoretical peak on 9 V stays above the limit and the copper was not DRC-checked. (4) One 47 ohm resistor per clock branch instead of the single R41: it removes overshoot and edge reversals in the model, but needs a layout change and one more placement. None of the four is needed for the order |

### Review round 1, second part (2026-11-21): local-editing package, order package, two software findings

After the table above was written, three more things were examined. An independent agent tested the local-editing
package of milestone 3 in a fresh clone with the real `build.sh` (findings T-01 to T-11: two high, one medium, eight
low). A second one reviewed the order package - the Gerber archive, the JLCPCB BOM and the CPL - with its own
scripts (OP-1 to OP-7: no high finding, two medium, five low). It found no part that is placed backwards, rotated
or on the wrong side and no BOM line that names a wrong part, and it checked the stock of all 44 BOM lines live
between 10:05 and 10:07 UTC: no line is below twice the need. That check is the reviewer's own and is not one of
the two reads of the prices. And the two software findings went to their second checkers: SW-1 was partly confirmed,
SW-2 was confirmed. Every finding was fixed or decided; the first table says for each one what is in the tree now.
The fixes were then tested by three further independent agents, each on a fresh clone with the patch applied. None
of them found a high or medium problem; their low findings are in the second table. The outputs in the repository
were regenerated after the fixes, on 2026-11-21 between 15:22 and 15:25 UTC (date lines of the ERC and DRC reports
and of the Gerber files): ERC 0 errors / 0 warnings; DRC 0 violations / 0 unconnected / 0 footprint errors;
4 warnings in the waived-check report (U2 type; J4, J6, C13 library comparison). The identifiers in brackets are
the reviewers' own (T, OP, SW; P, V and SWV in the tests of the fixes). Nothing was tested on hardware or on
Windows. Where a window of KiCad was needed, KiCad 10.0.8 ran on a virtual display (Xvfb) on Linux and was operated
by a script, not by a person. In the column "How it was tested" the runs are those of the agent that made the fix
and of the independent tester, each in its own clone of the repository.

| # | Finding (severity) | Decided and done: the state of the tree | How it was tested | What remains |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-8 | **The guard let edits pass when its record was missing, empty or lacked a line (high, T-01).** With `gen/generated.sha256` deleted, a text changed in a sheet was overwritten by `build.sh sch` with exit code 0 and without a message. The step then wrote a record of 41 lines without the board, so an edit of the board was overwritten by the next `build.sh pcb` as well. The same happened with one missing line and with an empty record. K-11 had named the missing record as a limit that ends with the next generating step; for the files of the other step that was wrong | Fail closed. Every existing file that a step is going to write must have a line in the record. A file without one is listed as `not recorded` and the step stops, unless `OVERWRITE=1` is set; a missing record therefore stops every generating step. The committed record is complete (42 lines), so a fresh clone builds without `OVERWRITE=1` | Five cases with the real `build.sh` in a copy of the built tree (record deleted, emptied, or without the line of the board or of one sheet, each with an edit): exit code 1 every time, 41 / 1 / 1 / 42 / 1 files named, nothing written, edit kept. The independent test repeated them in 8 runs with the same result and compared the SHA-256 sums of all files under `hardware/` and `fab/` before and after. Fresh clone: `build.sh all` twice with exit code 0 and no guard message | A kill during the write of the record itself was not provoked (R-18) |
| R-9 | **A file saved while a generating step ran was taken as generated (high, T-02).** `record` stored the sum of whatever was on disk at the end of a step. Seen with `build.sh all` on an unchanged tree: `voxhub.kicad_pro` was changed while the `pcb` step ran its DSN export; the build ended with exit code 0 and no message, the change was gone and the record matched the disk. Rated high because the loss is silent; it needs a save during the build, which runs about 3.5 minutes with `SKIP_ROUTE=1` | (1) Each generator command of a step is followed by `record` for the files it wrote, and each further command is preceded by a check. Files the step has already written are checked without regard to `OVERWRITE`. Nothing is recorded after a failed generator command, and the project file is recorded only when it equals `gen/project_settings.json` (measured on the way: pcbnew of KiCad 10.0.8 does not write the project file when it saves a board; `build_pcb.py` copies the settings itself). (2) Every check stops while a lock file `hardware/~*.lck` exists, i.e. while KiCad has the project open; `OVERWRITE=1` does not skip this stop. KiCad 10.0.8 writes `~voxhub.kicad_pro.lck` for the project manager and for each editor and `~voxhub.kicad_sch.lck` and `~voxhub.kicad_pcb.lck` for the editors, removes them when it is closed and leaves them behind after a crash; a file left behind can be deleted. (3) `build.sh outputs` writes no project file any more (R-11). (4) Added beyond the decision: while a generating step runs, the record has one more line, a sum of 64 zeros with the name `build step sch` or `build step pcb`. It is written after the first check has passed and removed after the last generator command. A step that was stopped, or one of whose generator commands failed, leaves the line behind, and the next run of that step (and `all`) stops with `unfinished` until `OVERWRITE=1` is set. A record with that line must not be committed | Saves timed against the real `build.sh`: 6 of 6 saves between two generator commands stop the step with exit code 1, the file is named as `changed`, the save stays on disk and is not recorded; the independent test, which replaced the file at moments taken from file-system events, got 7 of 7. Lock files: 24 of 24 runs with hand-made lock files stop (3 names x 4 steps x `OVERWRITE` 0 and 1), 32 of 32 in the independent test; with the KiCad project manager open on the virtual display 8 of 8 generating runs stop, and `build.sh outputs` runs with exit code 0 while KiCad is open and writes none of the 47 project files | One window remains, by decision (R-30): a save that lands while a single board-writing generator command runs is lost without a message. `pcb` and `route` do not look at edited sheets (R-14). A save from a KiCad window during a running build was not tried; the saves were simulated by replacing the file. kicad-cli itself holds `~voxhub.kicad_pro.lck` while it runs, so a second `build.sh` in the same checkout stops with the lock message |
| R-10 | **Files written through Docker belonged to root (medium, T-03).** After `build.sh all` in a checkout of a normal user, the board, the 33 footprint files, `outputs/gerbers` with its 16 files, the STEP file and `gen/work` belonged to root. `kicad-cli jobset run` as that user then ended with exit code 6 and left the old Gerbers in place, while both destinations still reported all jobs as succeeded. Reproduced on Linux; that a checkout inside WSL2 behaves the same was the reviewer's inference, not a test | The container still runs as root. At the end of every container call, also a failed or stopped one, the files under `hardware/` and `fab/` that do not belong to the owner of `hardware/` are given to that owner; when the owner is root nothing is done | As a normal user on Linux, by both testers with different user ids: `build.sh all` exit code 0, then 0 of 1103 entries of the checkout with another owner; `kicad-cli jobset run` as that user: exit code 0, 16 of 16 files in `outputs/gerbers` new; after a failing step, after Ctrl-C and after `docker kill`: 0 foreign files each time | Not tested: a checkout on a Windows drive (`/mnt/c`, Docker Desktop with a Windows path), rootless Docker, Podman, user-namespace remapping, and a checkout whose owner is not the user who runs `build.sh` |
| R-11 | **A killed waived-check run left `voxhub.kicad_pro` changed (low, T-04).** K-12 had named the case. Reproduced: container killed 2 s into the waived-check DRC of `build.sh outputs`; `build.sh` ended with exit code 137, the project file kept the two waived checks as warnings, the next `outputs` run stopped at its first DRC with 4 violations and no hint, and the generating steps offered `OVERWRITE=1`, which would have thrown away the owner's own project settings | `build.sh outputs` runs that DRC in a copy of the project under `gen/work/waived/` (project file, board, symbol library, footprints, sheets, and the rules file and library tables when present), switches the two checks to warnings in the copy and deletes the copy afterwards. `outputs` never writes the owner's project file, and the mechanism that put a saved original back is gone | Same kill, by both testers: exit code 137, all 47 project files unchanged (the independent test compared sum, inode and time stamps), both waived checks still ignored in the project file, the copy left behind with 14 entries and not shown by `git status`. `outputs` again: exit code 0, copy gone, 4 warnings in the report. Further `outputs` runs with a real short on the board and with an owner-edited project file: 0 project files changed | Nothing known |
| R-12 | **Ctrl-C did not stop a build step (low, T-05; older than the package).** SIGINT to the process group of `build.sh pcb`: 3 s later script and container still ran, and the container finished its step 22 s later and wrote the board and the record. A step could only be stopped with `docker kill` | Decided was `docker run --init`. That was not done: measured, `--init` alone stopped neither script nor container within 15 s of a SIGINT. Instead `build.sh` starts the container (and the Freerouting `java`) in the background and waits; traps for SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP end the container by its name, remove a lock file that the killed kicad-cli left, give the files back (R-10) and exit with 130, 143 or 129. The script prints that it was stopped by a signal, and a generating step that was cut is reported as `unfinished` by the next run (R-9) | Eight signal cases by each tester - Ctrl-C written to a pseudo terminal by a script, SIGINT to the process group, SIGTERM, SIGHUP, in the `sch`, `pcb`, `route` and `outputs` steps: `build.sh` ended 0.6 to 0.9 s after the signal, the container within 0.4 s, and no container, lock file or foreign file was left. `docker stop` and `docker kill` end the step too (exit codes 143 and 137). After a stop inside the placement the next `pcb`, `route` and `all` stop with `unfinished`, and `outputs` stops at its DRC with exit code 5, because the board is incomplete (true when the placement had already written the board, about 23 s into the step; before that `outputs` works on the board of the last finished run - review round 2, R-41) | Not tested: stopping a real Freerouting run (only a stand-in `java` that sleeps), signals with `KICAD_NATIVE=1`, a person pressing Ctrl-C at a terminal. A SIGKILL of `build.sh` cannot be caught: the container then runs on until its command ends. A signal in the first second of a step can leave the line of R-9 although no generator command had started (5 of 8 early signals); the next run then asks for `OVERWRITE=1` |

The reviewer concluded that the guard would report a save that changed nothing, and proposed not to write the line; a save from the schematic editor itself was not part of that test | Not done as decided. The decision was to leave the line out in `gen/kisch.py`. A save from the KiCad 10.0.8 schematic editor writes exactly that line: with the line left out, the editor put it back and the guard reported `changed voxhub.kicad_sch` after a save that had changed nothing. So the generator keeps the line, as K-2 describes it, and the guard accepts the root sheet with and without it | Both testers, with KiCad 10.0.8 windows on the virtual display, operated by a script (select all, delete, undo, save): the schematic editor rewrote the 6 sheets and the board editor the board, and the content of 0 of 42 generated files changed. `kicad-cli sch upgrade --force`, `sym upgrade`, `fp upgrade` and a load and save with pcbnew: 41 files rewritten, 1 changed, the root sheet by that one line; the guard returns 0 on that state | No form of the root sheet is byte-identical under both the editor and `kicad-cli sch upgrade`. (c) The record is written to `gen/generated.sha256.tmp` and renamed. (b), (d), (e), (f), (g): no change | (a) Ten values other than 1, among them `1; touch INJECTED` and `$(touch INJECTED)`, each with a sheet edit: exit code 1, nothing written, no file created; the independent test ran 13 values in 26 runs with the same result. (c) New inode after `record`, no temporary file left | (b) The list is now printed at every check that still finds the files. (d) While the line of R-9 is present, `sha256sum -c` reports that line as well. (e) After a failed `outputs` the Gerbers and the archive under `fab/jlcpcb` are those of the run before: look at the exit code before uploading. (f) and (g) are untested |

| R-26 | **Two-device path: the constant level of MK2 costs wake word detections for quiet speech (medium, SW-1; partly confirmed by a second agent with its own code).** W-1 and other documents said that wake word and voice activity detection do not depend on the level.
