| 13 | **OS full build runs on the host as an unprivileged user; github.com "archive" sources are fetched with git** (`VOXHUB_GITHUB_VIA_GIT=1`) | HAOS's `scripts/enter.sh` and Buildroot refuse to run as root; this environment's egress proxy answers 403 to `github.com/<owner>/<repo>/archive/...` while git access to the same public repositories is allowed. Buildroot still checks every tarball against its hash. Details in os/README.md. |
| 14 | **Showcase animation rendered with Cycles on the CPU at 4 samples + OpenImageDenoise, 21 s** | No GPU in the build machine: EEVEE through software OpenGL needs about 100 s per frame, Cycles about 9 s on average with these settings (1260 frames in 3 h 10 min). Details in media/README.md. |
| 15 | **Mouser data is not live** | mouser.com answers every automated request with HTTP 503 (bot protection) and no API key was available; evading the protection was not attempted. The BOM carries DigiKey live data plus clearly marked search-snippet values for Mouser. |
| 16 | **OS full build restarted after a self-inflicted stop (attempt 5)**; the four affected Buildroot packages are rebuilt, nothing is patched | Attempt 4 stopped after 3 h at `libblockdev` with `libtool: error: '.../build/util-linux-2.40.4/libuuid.la' is not a valid libtool archive`. Cause: the disk-space helper of this session (outside the repository) had emptied the build directories of finished packages, but the staging `libfdisk.la` / `libmount.la` (and `libdns.la`, `libgnutlsxx.la`) refer back into them. Rewriting the `.la` files would have hidden the mistake in a non-standard build tree, so the directories of `util-linux`, `host-util-linux`, `gnutls` and `dhcp` were deleted (Buildroot rebuilds them) and the helper now skips every directory that a libtool archive points to and anything below 150 MB. The run log is in os/README.md. |
| 17 | **Independent review of all A1 deliverables before calling them done**: five reviewers (USB lengths re-computed with their own parser, schematic / OS / assistant consistency against the data sheets, BOM and sourcing, documentation and enclosure, build scripts), each finding then attacked by a second agent | 51 findings, 48 confirmed, 3 refuted. The confirmed ones are fixed or, where a fix needs hardware or a production decision, documented (rows 18-24, Open items). The reason for doing it: the first A1 commit claimed a USB result that a plain re-computation disproved. |
| 18 | **USB pairs re-done: ESD parts turned into a flow-through orientation and swapped left / right, strict pad-to-pad router rules, contact-accurate length measurement** (section 6a) | Port 2 was really 4.9 mm off (limit 3.8 mm) although the report said 0.00 mm: the length measurement followed a loop. New result 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00 mm, 0 vias, no stubs, confirmed by an independent computation. |
| 19 | **C10 (220 nF) and C11 (1 uF VCC) get fixed positions directly at the LMR33640 pins** (1.8 mm, were 8.4 mm and 6 mm away; superseded by the complete hand placement of row 28) | The data sheet demands the 220 nF bypass "as close as possible to the regulator"; the greedy placer had put the larger capacitors first. Review finding. The rest of the power stage is still auto-placed (Open items). |
| 20 | **New autorouter session; Freerouting is started up to three times** and the session file is checked | Freerouting 2.0.1 twice routed the board and then left an empty session file (busy machine; the passes in its log were identical to a good run). `build.sh` used to copy that empty file over the checked-in session. |
| 21 | **Radio UART is `/dev/ttyAMA4`, not `ttyAMA1`**; openocd reset recipe changed to push-pull | The kernel HAOS 18.5 pins has fixed serial aliases (`serial4 = &uart4`). With `srst_open_drain` openocd never drives GPIO24 high, so the active-high reset would never be asserted. Review findings; text only, no hardware change. |
| 22 | **Build scripts fail loudly**: `build.sh outputs` stops on DRC violations and when a USB pair is unmatched or untraceable; `postroute.sh` treats a missing measurement as failed and gives up when the repair loop stops making progress; `os/build-image.sh` checks for root before creating anything, makes `--out` absolute, writes git-made tarballs under a temporary name and aborts before the build when a source could not be fetched or has the wrong hash | Review findings: each of these paths could end "successfully" with a wrong or missing result. |
| 23 | **BOM honesty fixes**: Mouser part numbers that were only built from Mouser's prefix scheme are marked as such (18 of 37); unit prices keep four decimals so that quantity x price reproduces the extended price; the 220 nF line says that DigiKey shows no stock figure; the Cal-Chip alternate is described as what it is (2,000-piece Marketplace minimum); crystal stability figures corrected | Review findings. Totals did not change. |
| 24 | **Section 9 completed**: crystal row verified against the Epson-hosted brief, 2N7002 row added (mirror of the Nexperia sheet), generic passives row now says plainly that no manufacturer document was read | Review finding: README claimed "every row verified" while two rows were not. |
| 25 | **OS host build: Buildroot's `host-libyaml` is built first when the host has `libyaml-dev`** | Attempt 5 of the full build stopped in U-Boot's host dtc: it found the host's `/usr/include/yaml.h` but not the library through Buildroot's pkg-config. Upstream host leak that the HAOS builder container does not have; the script now works around it instead of telling the user to uninstall a package. |
| 26 | **`VOXHUB_GOPROXY`: optional Go module proxy for the OS build** | Attempt 6 stopped at the last Go package: Buildroot fetches Go modules from their own hosts (`GOPROXY=direct`) and gopkg.in is not reachable from this environment. The option rewrites that one setting in the cloned Buildroot tree; integrity checks (go.sum, Buildroot's tarball hash) are unchanged. Opt-in, like the git fallback for GitHub tarballs. |
| 27 | **OS host build: the RAUC signing key paths of the HAOS scripts are redirected from `/build` to the cloned tree**; the disk-space helper of the session is switched off | Attempt 8 stopped in `post-build.sh` (`Can't open "/build/key.pem"`): `/build` only exists inside the HAOS builder container. Attempt 7 had stopped one step earlier because the helper had emptied the python3 build directory that Buildroot needs for the final `.pyc` compilation - the second self-inflicted stop, so the helper went. **Attempt 9 completed**: image and OTA bundle built and inspected (os/README.md). |
| 28 | **Buck power stage placed by hand**: U2 turned by 180 degrees (VIN / PGND towards the PD stage, SW / BOOT / VCC towards the inductor), C10 + C8 + C9 in one pour with the VIN pin, C12 and C11 1.8 mm from BOOT / SW and VCC, inductor under the SW pin, output capacitors below it | The second review round found that the fix of row 19 had a side effect: with the inductor on the VIN side the switch-node track ran between C8 and the regulator, cut C8 off from the VIN pour and left it on a 20 mm long 0.4 mm track round the inductor pad. Placing single parts was treating symptoms; the greedy placer is the wrong tool for a switching converter. USB result unchanged (0.00 mm, no vias), build reproducible. What is still automatic and weak (the 0.4 mm feed from Q1) is listed under Open items. |
| 29 | **Second review round on the fixes** (four re-checkers + four verifiers): 47 of the 48 confirmed findings of round one fixed, one (bom-1) partly - its leftover sentence is among the new findings and is corrected now; the USB result re-computed a third time independently and confirmed; 17 new findings confirmed (one medium: row 28; the rest wording, counts and script robustness), 5 refuted | A fix can introduce the next defect - row 28 is the proof. Fixed in the same pass: the settle loop now gives up on a repeated violation list instead of equal counts and never reads a stale DRC report; `build.sh` checks for `zip`; `build-image.sh` refuses a clone of another HAOS version and Wi-Fi options in full mode; `build_showcase.sh` discards frames rendered from other inputs; counts and dates in the texts corrected. |
| 30 | **Third, focused round on the power-stage change** (three checkers + three verifiers) **- and the decision to stop changing the board**: the split-pour defect is gone, the USB figures were confirmed with a fourth parser, the outputs belong to the committed board; 12 new findings confirmed, all low | What they found on the board is layout polish, not a defect of the prototype: the ground side of the buck is still the scripted fan-out, the VBUS pour has 0.4 mm necks, two fan-out vias lie in QFN lead pads, two silkscreen outlines cross a text. Each further re-spin so far has produced the next finding of the round after it and makes the rendered video stale again, so these are documented with numbers as "before a production spin" instead of being fixed now. The text and script findings (waived-check reasons, via-in-pad note, review counts, fingerprint of the render inputs, two unreachable error messages) are fixed. |
| 31 | **Video re-rendered from the final board - only the 866 frames that show the PCB** (150 to 1015); the 394 closed-device frames of the first render are kept | A full render takes 3 h 10 min, the PCB frames 1 h 54 min. The kept frames cannot differ: shell and base are unchanged (STL byte-identical) and the board is inside the closed case. Checked instead of assumed: five kept frames rendered again from the final board match to within 3 of 255 on single pixels, and there is no jump at the two joins. `build_showcase.sh` itself always renders one consistent set (input fingerprint). |

## 0. Constraints of the environment this was designed in

The first session ran in a network-restricted sandbox. These blocks shaped several decisions (the table is kept as a
record; the second session had general internet access and re-did the checks, see the log above and section 9; the
KiCad 9 tooling named in it was replaced in the fifth session by KiCad 10.0.8 in the project's own image
`voxhub-kicad:10.0` and by frozen copies of the library parts, log K-1 to K-3):

| Blocked | Consequence | Next-best option used |
|---|---|---|
| KiCad PPA (`ppa.launchpadcontent.net`), `downloads.kicad.org`; Ubuntu 24.04 only ships KiCad 7 | no native KiCad >= 8 | KiCad **9.0.9** from the official Docker image `kicad/kicad:9.0`, pulled through `mirror.gcr.io` (Docker Hub itself was rate-limited, GHCR blob host blocked). All `kicad-cli`/`pcbnew` steps run in that container. |
| Manufacturer sites (ti.com, infineon.com, st.com, raspberrypi.com datasheets, ...) | datasheets could not be opened directly | Pin-outs and land patterns taken from the KiCad 9 standard libraries (which cite the datasheets), from datasheet PDFs/text mirrored in public GitHub repos, from open hardware reference designs (official CM4IO KiCad library, SparkFun Thing Plus Matter, DNMS), and from web-search summaries. Each fact is tagged with its source in section 9. |
| Distributor sites (digikey.com, mouser.com, arrow.com), Octopart | no live stock/price lookup | Web-search snapshots of distributor listings (undated, sometimes conflicting). Lifecycle status is reliable; stock and price are indicative only - re-check before ordering. |
| `sources.buildroot.net`, `kernel.org`, `gnu.org`, `deb.debian.org`, ... | a from-source HAOS/Buildroot build cannot download its ~400 source tarballs | The default image is produced by repacking the **official** HAOS release (GitHub release assets were reachable). The from-source path is implemented and was run until its first blocked download - see `os/README.md`. |
| KiCad 3D model library is not in the Docker image | no models for renders | Individual STEP files fetched from `gitlab.com/kicad/libraries/kicad-packages3D`; a few are missing upstream and are replaced by simple CadQuery stand-ins. |

What was still blocked in the second session (2026-11-11):

| Blocked | Consequence | What was done |
|---|---|---|
| mouser.com (HTTP 503 for every automated request), octopart / trustedparts / findchips (403) | no live Mouser stock or prices | DigiKey is live for every line; Mouser part numbers and figures come from search-engine snippets, 18 of the 37 part numbers are only built from Mouser's prefix scheme or carried over from the first session - both kinds are marked in the BOM ("NOT live" / "not seen on mouser.com"). A Mouser API key would close this. |
| silabs.com (MGM240P), hirose.com (DF40, DM3AT), littelfuse.com (C&K JS, SMBJ), analog.com (ADAU7002, MAX98357A), st.com (USBLC6-2), nexperia.com (2N7002) - 403 / unreachable | nine documents could not be read from the manufacturer's own server | the manufacturer's PDF was read from a mirror (SparkFun, distributor document servers); revision and mirror URL are listed per part in section 9 |
| `github.com/<owner>/<repo>/archive/...` tarballs (403 from the egress proxy; git access to the same repositories works) | 37 source packages of the HAOS build cannot be downloaded the normal way | `os/build-image.sh` fetches them with git when `VOXHUB_GITHUB_VIA_GIT=1` (section 7) |
| no GPU, 4 CPU cores, 30 GB disk | render time and OS build are the bottlenecks | Cycles at 4 samples + denoiser for the animation; build directories of finished Buildroot packages are pruned during the OS build |

## 1. Architecture: SoM instead of a discrete SoC

**Raspberry Pi Compute Module 4 Lite, wireless (CM4104000, 4 GB)** on a custom carrier.

- Home Assistant OS has an official `rpi4-64` board target that boots unmodified on a CM4, so the SD image
  comes from the official HAOS release/build system - no port. (The CM4 is also what Home Assistant Yellow uses.)
- The module already contains the hard parts: BCM2711, LPDDR4, PMIC, and a certified
  2.4/5 GHz 802.11ac + Bluetooth 5.0 radio with antenna. A discrete BCM2711/RK3566 design would need
  0.4 mm BGA fan-out, DDR4 routing and RF certification - not something to generate from scripts and call manufacturable.
- "Lite" (no eMMC) because the requirement is microSD storage; the carrier provides the socket.
- Second sources: any CM4 Lite wireless variant is a drop-in (CM4102000 2 GB, CM4108000 8 GB).
  The CM5 uses the same connectors but a different HAOS target (`rpi5-64`), 5 V/5 A supply and
  different USB pins, so it is **not** a drop-in for this carrier.
- Production lifetime: Raspberry Pi commits to CM4 production until at least January 2034.

Rejected: Home Assistant Green SoC (RK3566, needs full DDR/PMIC design), ODROID modules (no HAOS SoM target),
CM5 (higher power, would need 5 V/5 A and has no advantage for a cloud-LLM voice box).

## 2. Power

- **USB-C PD sink: Infineon CYPD3177 (EZ-PD BCR)**. Fully autonomous, configured by resistor straps - no
  firmware and no NVM programming step in production (an STUSB4500 would boot at 5 V until its NVM is
  programmed; FUSB302 needs a host MCU that is not powered yet).
- **Negotiated voltage: 9 V minimum, 20 V maximum, 2 A minimum.** The theoretical simultaneous peak on the 5 V
  rail is 3.45 A = 17.3 W, i.e. about 19.6 W at the connector: 17.3 W plus about 2.3 W of losses in the regulator,
  the inductor and the load switch, which is 2.17 A at 9 V or 0.98 A at 20 V (power budget in README, corrected
  figures in log N-3). Typical operation is 4.95 W on the rail and 5.2 to 5.4 W at the connector; realistic operation
  stays below 12 W.
  5 V/3 A (15 W) is not enough, so 5 V is excluded; 9 V/2 A = 18 W is the smallest PDO that is accepted - it covers
  realistic operation and practically every >= 20 W PD charger offers it, but the theoretical peak is 9 % above it,
  and on a 9 V contract the load switch Q1 limits the 5 V rail to about 12 W (see "Losses and temperature" below).
  Charger advice since review round 1 (log N-2, R-5): for loads above about 12 W - both USB ports feeding 500 mA
  devices, for example - use a charger with a fixed 12 V, 15 V or 20 V profile of at least 2 A, in practice 30 W or
  more, or a 27 W unit with 12 V / 2.25 A. A charger whose highest profile of 2 A or more is 9 V is for loads up to
  about 12 W only.
  **PDO selection (verified):** the BCR walks through the source PDOs from the highest voltage down and takes the first
  one inside VBUS_MIN..VBUS_MAX that offers at least ISNK_COARSE + ISNK_FINE (CY4533 kit guide 002-25263 *B, section
  "PDO selection"; datasheet 002-25383 *C section 4.1). A 30 W charger with 20 V / 1.5 A, 15 V / 2 A, 9 V / 3 A therefore
  ends up at 15 V. If no PDO qualifies it requests 5 V / 900 mA with the capability-mismatch flag, drives FAULT high
  and leaves VBUS_FET_EN off.
- **Straps (values of revision A3, log C-2):** VBUS_MIN = 9 V with R1 / R2 = 12k / 2.2k (511 mV at the pin, 503 to
  520 mV with 1 % resistors; the 9 V level is 249 to 786 mV), VBUS_MAX = 20 V with R3 = 1k to VDDD (level from
  2779 mV), ISNK_COARSE = 2 A with R4 / R5 = 2.2k / 1k (1031 mV, 1017 to 1046 mV; the 2 A level is 787 to 1347 mV),
  ISNK_FINE = +0 mA with R6 = 1k to ground (level 0 to 248 mV). These are no longer the resistor values of the
  datasheet tables, which the datasheet calls "suggested": they were verified against the pin voltage windows that
  the same tables publish for every level (002-25383 Rev. *C, tables 2 to 4; voltages for VDDD = 3.3 V, read by an
  8-bit ADC with about 52 mV total error; smallest margin 230 mV). Review round 1 recomputed all straps
  independently; for R1 / R2 the margins to the window limits are 254 and 266 mV. The dividers draw 1.26 mA from
  VDDD continuously (A2: 0.98 mA); while FAULT is high it is about 2.7 mA, because the red LED D4 is fed from the
  FAULT pin through R7 = 1k. The rating of the controller's internal 3.3 V regulator is not published; for a next
  revision D4 should be fed from VBUS through a FET as in Infineon's kit.
  FLIP keeps its 49.9k pull-up (R8): the controller reads that value as "port not data capable", which this
  power-only port is.
- **Load switch**: AO3401A P-FET (Q1), gate driven by the BCR's `VBUS_FET_EN` (active-low, slew-controlled driver that
  swings 0..VBUS) through R9 / R10 = 100k / 100k, so VGS = -0.5 x VBUS: 3.56 V at the lowest valid sink voltage of a
  9 V contract (7.30 V), 4.4 to 4.5 V at exactly 9 V, 10.0 V at exactly 20 V and at most 10.86 V at 21.5 V; the
  part allows +-12 V. With the tolerance of the contract voltage a healthy board shows 4.2 to 4.8 V at a 9 V
  contract, 5.6 to 6.4 V at 12 V, 6.9 to 8.0 V at 15 V and 9.0 to 10.6 V at 20 V (there the lower values mean that
  D5 is clamping, which is normal).
  From 23.8 V on VBUS the divider alone would exceed 12 V, so the 10 V Zener D5 between VBUS and the gate is a working
  part of the circuit and must stay populated. It starts to conduct between 18.8 and 21.2 V on VBUS: it carries a few
  tens of uA at a 20 V contract (at most 14 uA at exactly 20 V) and 171 uA at the 35.5 V clamp of the TVS.
  C7 = 100 nF 16 V between VBUS and the gate slows the turn-on: the simulated surge into the input capacitors at a
  20 V contract is about 3 A (3.3 A in the authors' simulation, 2.7 to 3.7 A in the reviewer's; typical transfer
  curve of the FET, driver taken as a step; not measured). Its rate of change is inside the USB PD limit; its peak
  is still above the requested 2 A for about 50 us (about 2 A at 15 V, below 1 A at 9 V), which is accepted (log
  N-6). Turn-off takes 6 to 13 ms with the controller driving the pin (about 10 ms at 20 V) and up to 30 ms when the
  controller is unpowered; the regulator may re-enable a few times while the switch fades out. Nothing behind the
  switch needs a fast turn-off. At a 9 V contract the gate voltage is nominally at the datasheet's 4.5 V point, where
  RDS(on) is at most 60 mOhm at 25 C; the A2 divider gave 3.6 V, where only the 85 mOhm limit of the 2.5 V point
  applies. Revision A2 had 100k / 150k and 10 nF (-0.4 x VBUS, simulated surge 7.2 A); log C-3 and N-6. The first
  schematic revision had an N-FET inverter here - wrong polarity, caught when the datasheet facts were cross-checked.
  A single FET is enough: the board is sink-only.
- If the charger cannot meet the request the BCR stays at 5 V, raises FAULT (red LED D4) and never closes the
  switch; additionally the buck's enable divider starts the regulator only above 7.39 V (7.08 V at the low tolerance
  corner), so nothing downstream starts at vSafe5V (5.5 V at most).
- **5 V rail: TI LMR33640ADDA** (36 V, 4 A, 400 kHz, HSOIC-8 PowerPAD). All external parts are the values of the
  datasheet table for 5 V / 400 kHz: 6.8 uH (Sunltech SLO0630H6R8MTT, Isat 8 A, Irms 4.5 A, 45/54 mOhm; A1: Bourns SRP7028A-6R8M, log A2-10), 4 x 22 uF out
  (the table's typical value; minimum 3 x 22 uF), 100k / 24.9k feedback (5.02 V), 2 x 10 uF / 50 V X7R + 220 nF / 50 V
  X7R in, 100 nF boot, 1 uF VCC (the 50 V X7R part since revision A3, log C-5). Output 4.86 / 5.02 / 5.17 V over the
  tolerances of reference and resistors, up to 5.23 V at light load. After DC bias the four 22 uF at the regulator
  are 54 uF (Samsung typical data), the level of the table's minimum column; together with the four other 22 uF on
  the +5 V net the rail has 109 uF, so none of the eight may be removed or downgraded (log C-8). Inductor ripple
  0.82 A p-p at 9 V in, 1.38 A at 20 V. Worst-case peak inductor current at the 3.45 A budget: 4.51 A against the
  regulator's minimum current limit of 4.8 A (Isat 8 A); at the rated 4 A it would be 5.06 A, so the stage is not a
  guaranteed 4 A supply at 20 V input (log N-4). The 3.45 A budget counts both USB-A ports together at about 1 A
  (2 x 500 mA); the 1.3 A limit per port is short-circuit protection, not an allowance (section 3).
  EN divider R11 / R12 = 100k / 20k: on at 7.08 / 7.39 / 7.69 V, off at 6.49 / 6.79 / 7.08 V (min / typ / max; log
  C-4; A2 had 470k / 100k: 7.02 V and 6.45 V typical). The off values assume the typical 100 mV hysteresis; with
  70 mV the highest would be 7.26 V, and the datasheet gives no limit.
  Margins on a 9 V contract: at start-up 0.86 V (the unloaded source has at least 8.55 V, turn-on is at most
  7.69 V). Under load the lowest valid sink voltage is 7.30 V at the socket, minus 0.2 to 0.3 V in Q1 and copper,
  against a turn-off of up to 7.08 V: no margin at the theoretical peak, 0.34 V or more at 12 W, about 0.75 V in the
  static corner. With a long or thin cable (from about 0.5 ohm loop resistance) the regulator can therefore fall into
  a restart loop on a 9 V contract. That is not damage, and it concerns 9 V contracts only. Test 4 of
  `fab/ASSEMBLY_AND_TEST.md` checks it: on the 20 W charger with the module under load, VBUS_SW at C8 / C9 must stay
  above about 7.5 V.
  36 V rating leaves margin over 20 V + TVS clamp. Internally compensated -> few parts.
  Revision A was set to 5.1 V "to absorb drops"; that put the tolerance corners above the CM4's 5.25 V limit (see log no. 2).
- **Losses and temperature (log N-2, N-3, R-5):** by TI's efficiency curves the regulator dissipates 1.34 W (9 V in)
  to 1.63 W (20 V in) at the theoretical 3.45 A peak; the power stage with the 54 mOhm inductor is then about 89.7 %
  (9 V) and 88.4 % (20 V) efficient. These losses hold for a junction of about 70 C; at the junction temperature the
  model predicts they are about 1.6 to 1.9 W (TI Fig. 9-4). A conduction model of the board's copper (assumed cooling
  10 W/m2K on both faces, 50 C around the board) gives 37.5 K/W from the regulator's pad to ambient and then a
  junction of about 150 to 165 C at that peak, against the 125 C rating and the 150 C absolute maximum: at a sustained
  theoretical peak the regulator is expected to cycle through its thermal shutdown (165 C off, 148 C on). At the
  realistic maximum of 12 W the model gives about 100 to 113 C and at typical load about 76 C. The theoretical peak
  cannot be held continuously inside the closed enclosure.
  On a 9 V contract the load switch Q1 is the limiting part, and it has no thermal protection. The same model gives
  114 K/W from its drain pad to ambient (109 to 124 K/W for 15 to 5 W/m2K); the datasheet adds 63 typ / 80 max K/W
  from junction to lead, the neighbours add 24 K at 12 W and 44 K at the peak. Q1 dissipates 0.14 to 0.22 W at 12 W
  and 0.4 to 0.6 W at the theoretical peak (0.28 W is the figure for the 25 C limit of RDS(on)). Its junction is then
  at about 100 to 120 C at 12 W, 115 to 150 C at 13.9 W (both USB ports at 500 mA, a busy module, no sine tone) and
  above its 150 C absolute maximum at the theoretical peak. On a 12 / 15 / 20 V contract the theoretical peak gives
  128 to 144 / 116 to 124 / 110 to 113 C. So on a 9 V contract the 5 V rail has to stay at or below about 12 W; the
  30 W charger of the shopping list negotiates 15 V.
  All of this is a model, not a measurement, and it depends strongly on the assumed cooling (regulator at the 1.1 W
  loss the model was first run with: 124.5 C, and 114 to 151 C for 15 to 5 W/m2K). Measure the regulator, the
  inductor and Q1 on the first board, Q1 on a 9 V contract at about 12 W.
- **3.3 V**: taken from the CM4's own 3.3 V output (600 mA), exactly as the official CM4IO board does. This removes a
  regulator and all power-sequencing/back-powering questions. The loads add up to 163 mA typical and 403 mA at the
  peak (hub 95 / 110 mA with two ports, microSD 50 / 200 mA, radio 10 / 85 mA, microphones and converter 3 mA,
  pull-ups 5 mA; README, log N-3).
- TVS SMBJ22A on VBUS, 24 V ESD diodes on CC1/CC2. VBUS bypass in front of the switch: C1 + C2 = 2 x 1 uF 50 V X7R,
  1.78 uF at 5 V after DC bias (USB asks for 1 to 10 uF at attach; log C-5). Known margin: at its full rated surge
  (16.9 A) the SMBJ22A clamps at 35.5 V - below the LMR33640's 38 V, but above the 30 V absolute maximum of the
  CYPD3177 and the AO3401A. Hot-plug ringing is far below that current, so this has been accepted since revision A;
  TI TVS2200DRVR would be the tighter clamp (different footprint).

## 3. USB

- The CM4 exposes one USB 2.0 port -> **Microchip USB2514B** hub (the same hub the CM4IO uses), ordered since
  revision A3 as USB2514BT-I/M2 (C510949), the tape-and-reel ordering code of the same device (log F-1).
  Ports 1-2 go to a stacked dual USB-A connector (J6: Jing Extension 907-112A1022D10200 since revision A3, on the land
  pattern that was drawn for the Wurth 61400826021, which is now the alternate; log F-2); ports 3-4 are disabled by
  strapping D+/D- to 3.3 V through 10k.
- Per-port power: **TPS2553** (EN active high from the hub's PRTPWR, FAULT to OCS_N), limit 1.3 A typ.,
  100 uF polymer + 100 nF per port, **USBLC6-2SC6** ESD arrays. The limit is short-circuit protection, not an
  allowance: the power budget counts both USB-A ports together at about 1 A (2 x 500 mA). With both ports at their
  limit the 5 V rail is asked for 4.1 to 4.5 A without the amplifier and 4.8 to 5.2 A with it, which the regulator
  does not guarantee; the module then restarts, and a larger charger does not cure that (log N-4).
- No external EEPROM: internal default configuration via CFG_SEL straps (self-powered, individual port power).
  The strap pull-downs are 100k (the datasheet asks for 47 to 100 kOhm), the filter pins CRFILT / PLLFILT carry
  100 nF each (the datasheet's maximum; log C-9), and VBUS_DET is pulled to 3.3 V through R28 = 10k (100k up to
  revision A2: with the maximum input leakage the pin then had 0.21 V of margin to its 2.0 V threshold, now 1.12 V;
  log C-12).
- RESET_N comes from the CM4's nEXTRST output (as on the CM4IO board): the hub stays in reset until the SoC runs.
- The hub draws 95 mA typical / 110 mA maximum from 3.3 V with two ports enabled. Its exposed pad is its only ground
  connection and has two vias (log N-5).
- Current limits with the verified TPS2553 equation: 20k -> 1215 / 1295 / 1375 mA (min / typ / max at 25 C) per USB-A
  port, 49.9k -> 490 / 520 / 550 mA for the microSD supply; over temperature and with the 1 % resistors 1.19 to 1.39 A
  and 0.47 to 0.59 A. Known layout weakness, not changed in A3: the input capacitors C37 / C40 are 10.9 / 16.7 mm from
  the IN pins of U5 / U6 and the limit resistors R37 / R39 have 14 / 20 mm of track, where TI asks for "as close as
  possible" and "as short as possible"; the limit of the two USB-A ports can be slightly less accurate than computed
  (log N-1).
- Data lines (designed in revision A2, copper unchanged in A3; log A2-20 to A2-22): stack-up JLC04161H-3313; the
  upstream pair is a coupled 0.14 / 0.16 mm pair (89.96 ohm in JLCPCB's calculator), the downstream lines are two
  matched 0.185 mm lines per port (45.2 ohm each).
- Hub crystal: 24 MHz, CL 12 pF (YXC X322524MOB4SI) with 2 x 15 pF (log A2-14).

## 4. Audio

- **Microphones: 2 x Infineon IM72D128V01 (PDM) -> ADI ADAU7002 (PDM-to-I2S) -> CM4 I2S.**
  The first choice was the I2S-output TDK ICS-43434, but sourcing showed the whole I2S MEMS category is being
  discontinued (ICS-43434: last-time-buy passed; SPH0645LM4H: obsolete; CMM-4030D-261-I2S: obsolete; TDK T5848 is
  1.8 V-only with a different pin-out). PDM microphones are the actively produced mainstream, and the ADAU7002
  turns the pair into a standard stereo I2S stream at 3.3 V with zero configuration.
  Revisions A1 and A2 used the IM69D130V01, which Infineon lists as not for new designs and which fell to 43 pieces
  at JLCPCB. The IM72D128V01 (ordered as IM72D128V01XTMA1, C5119248) has the same land pattern (package PG-LLGA-5-3
  against PG-LLGA-5-1), pin-out, timing and -36 dBFS sensitivity and the same 2.9 to 3.3 MHz clock window (the other
  windows are narrower: the sound card must stay at 48 kHz, which the googlevoicehat codec driver enforces), 72
  instead of 69 dB(A) SNR, an overload point of 128 instead of 130 dBSPL and an absolute maximum of 3.6 V instead of
  4.0 V on any pin, with the 3.3 V rail at 3.38 V at most; log F-3.
  The clock reaches both microphones through one series resistor, R41, in a star: 43.3 mm of track to MK1 and
  94.1 mm to MK2. R41 is 75 ohm since review round 1 (log R-1); the 33 ohm before had been sized against the 4.0 V
  limit of the old part. Simulated peak at the microphone pins with 75 ohm, driver and input data assumed: 3.41 V
  nominal and 3.53 / 3.54 V in the fast corner at a 3.30 V rail, 3.62 / 3.63 V at the 3.3825 V top of the rail
  tolerance without track loss (3.55 V with it), against the 3.6 V limit; edges of at most 12.0 ns against the
  13 ns limit. Not removed: with a driver edge of 0.5 ns the rising edge at MK1 is not monotonic (a reversal of
  about 0.3 V inside the threshold band). Nothing of this is measured (open item 12).
  Two things follow from its datasheet. C43 / C44 at the supply pins are 1 uF (25 V X5R 0402) instead of 100 nF.
  And the microphone with SELECT = VDD - MK2, the right channel - puts a constant level of typically -40 dBFS into
  its data stream (MK1: -80 dBFS); the ADAU7002 has no high-pass filter and passes it on, about 1 % of full scale in
  the samples of the right channel. The VoxHub Assistant add-on removes it before the gain (log W-1). The two-device
  path passes it on: in a simulation that made no difference from 60 dBSPL at the microphone and cost wake word
  detections for quieter speech (4 to 14 percentage points at 45 to 55 dBSPL; log W-1, R-26). It was not tested on
  hardware.
  Speech is far below full scale in the samples. With -36 dBFS at 94 dBSPL and no gain in the ADAU7002, speech of
  50 / 60 / 70 dBSPL at the microphones is about 2.3 / 7.3 / 23 counts rms in the 16-bit capture (calculated). The
  gain is therefore set in software: a source volume of 4 in the two-device path (log S-9) and a default `mic_gain`
  of 32 in the VoxHub Assistant add-on (log R-27, section 8). Both are to be calibrated on the first board (open
  item 11).
  Alternates with the same footprint and pin-out: IM69D130V01 (the A2 part; its datasheet states no constant level)
  and IM73D122V01 (10 dB more sensitive, overload point 122 dBSPL - every gain setting would have to move: calculated
  with the factor 3.16 for 10 dB, the source volume 4 becomes about 1.3 and the default 32 about 10).
- **Amplifier: MAX98357A** (I2S in, filterless class D, 3.2 W / 4 ohm at 5 V, about 86 % efficient at that power)
  to a 2-pin JST-PH header.
- **Linux side needs no custom driver**: wiring matches the Google voiceHAT (BCLK GPIO18, LRCLK GPIO19,
  data-in GPIO20, data-out GPIO21, amp SD_MODE GPIO16), so the stock `googlevoicehat-soundcard` overlay gives one
  full-duplex ALSA card. Verified present (overlay + kernel modules) in the real HAOS 18.5 rpi4-64 image.
- **Hardware mute**: DPDT slide switch on the top face. Pole A lights a red LED (D9: KT-0603R through R22 = 1k from
  3.3 V, 1.44 mA; all LEDs are listed in section 6), pole B physically disconnects the
  converter's data line from the CM4 (and grounds the CM4 input). Software cannot override it. Cutting the
  microphone supply instead was rejected: the I2S/PDM clocks would back-power the microphones through their ESD diodes.
  The software only reads the switch: pole A also feeds GPIO27 through R23 = 10k, with R24 = 100k to ground. Since
  review round 1 the internal pull-down of that input is switched on as well (log R-3), because 100k alone left
  1.01 V at the pin with the module's maximum input leakage of 10 uA, above the 0.8 V low threshold. Now the level
  is at most 0.42 V unmuted and about 2.5 V muted (2.21 V in the worst case; the high threshold is 2.0 V). The mute
  itself and the red LED do not depend on this input.
- No acoustic echo cancellation (would need an XMOS-class DSP). The assistant is half-duplex instead.
- Microphone spacing 102 mm (diagonal: MK1 at 9.5 / 70.2 mm, MK2 at 92.0 / 10.0 mm on the board), bottom-port through
  the PCB, 7.6 mm acoustic path. The sound hole in the board is 0.8 mm, not plated; the ground ring of the land
  pattern around it runs from 0.61 to 1.04 mm radius since review round 1 (Infineon draws 0.58 to 1.04 mm), which
  keeps the copper 0.21 mm from the hole (log R-2, K-6).

## 5. Zigbee / Thread radio (nice-to-have, included)

- **RF-star RF-BM-2652P2** (TI CC2652P, +20 dBm PA, PCB antenna, 16.5 x 30 mm; since revision A2, log A2-3). A1 used the
  Silicon Labs MGM240PB32VNA3, which nobody stocks. CC2652P coordinators are first-class in Home Assistant (ZHA radio
  type "ZNP", Zigbee2MQTT adapter "zstack") and the same module runs an OpenThread RCP.
- UART4 on GPIO8 (TXD4 -> module DIO12 = RX) and GPIO9 (RXD4 <- DIO13 = TX), `dtoverlay=uart4` -> `/dev/ttyAMA4` (the
  Raspberry Pi kernel that HAOS 18.5 pins has fixed serial aliases, `serial4 = &uart4`). No flow control: Koenkk's
  firmware does not use it. GPIO10 / GPIO11 stay wired to DIO18 / DIO19 for a firmware that would.
- Reset: GPIO24 drives an open-drain 2N7002 on the module's RESET pin (**GPIO24 high = radio held in reset**), 100k
  pull-up to 3.3 V on the reset line, 100k gate pull-down. The SoC's boot-time pull-down on GPIO24 keeps the FET off,
  i.e. the radio runs by default.
- Boot-loader backdoor: module DIO15 on GPIO25, active low, 2.2k pull-up (log A2-5). Low while reset is released starts
  the CC2652P ROM serial boot loader on the same UART.
- JTAG: TMS GPIO22, TCK GPIO23, TDO (DIO16) GPIO17, TDI (DIO17) GPIO7 - the recovery path when a module arrives with a
  firmware whose backdoor is disabled (OpenOCD bit-bang, see `tools/voxhub-cc2652-jtag.cfg`). TMS / TCK / reset / TDO
  are also on the Tag-Connect pads J8 for a TI XDS110.
- The module is delivered without the Zigbee firmware this project uses: `tools/flash_zigbee.sh` writes it from the
  CM4 with one command (log A2-32, procedure in `fab/ASSEMBLY_AND_TEST.md`).
- In the EU the 2.4 GHz band is limited to 10 dBm/MHz power spectral density, so a Zigbee coordinator has to run at
  about +10 dBm whatever the module can do; the +20 dBm amplifier is headroom, not range.

## 6. Mechanical concept

- PCB 100 x 74 mm, 4 layers, 1.6 mm. All connectors on the rear edge.
- The PCB hangs **component side down** under the top cover. That gives the bottom-port microphones a short path to
  the top surface, puts the status LEDs and the mute slider on the side that faces the cover, and lets
  the whole shell print upside down without supports.
- Speaker (enclosed, 70 x 30 x 17 mm, 4 ohm, 3 W, JST-PH plug) sits next to the PCB, fires upwards, and is clamped by a
  pedestal on the base plate (log A2-30).
- CM4 antenna end at the board edge (1 mm from it), copper keep-out on all layers under the antenna as drawn in the
  CM4 datasheet (log no. 1); no enclosure screw or other metal within 10 mm of it except the module's own mounting screw.
  Radio module at the front edge with its antenna end on the edge; no copper on any layer under the antenna
  (board 11.75..31.25 x 68.0..74.0, the area RF-star's drawing marks plus a margin).
- No Ethernet, HDMI, camera, PCIe: not required; leaving them out keeps the board routable on two signal layers.
  Consequence: first-boot network setup is done by pre-seeding Wi-Fi credentials into the image (`os/build-image.sh --wifi-ssid`).
- No fan: 14 x 14 x 7 mm heat sink on the SoC plus vents; see thermal estimate in README.
- User button dropped (wake word + mute switch cover the UI); debug UART header footprint is DNP.
- LEDs (parts and values of revision A3, log C-6 and C-7): three part numbers. Red Hubei KENTO KT-0603R: D4 (PD
  fault, R7 = 1k from the controller's FAULT pin) and D9 (muted, R22 = 1k from 3.3 V) at about 1.4 mA (D9: 0.99 to
  1.86 mA), about 11 to 27 mcd. Green KT-0603G: the status LED D7 and the power LED D6, each through 10k from 5 V
  (R18, R15; 0.27 mA, about 10 to 55 mcd - this current is below the range of the datasheet curve), and the
  activity LED D10 through R26 = 1k from 3.3 V to CM4 pin 21, the circuit of the CM4 IO board: 0.9 mA typical,
  about 0.1 mA with every limit against it. By the datasheet curves D10 lights on every board, but its brightness
  varies about 10 : 1 between boards; a minimum current cannot be derived from published limits. Blue KT-0603B:
  the status LED D8 through R20 = 2.2k from 5 V, 23 to 57 mcd. All intensities are read from the typical curves of
  the datasheets and scaled to the bin limits; they are estimates, and below about 1 mA they are extrapolations
  (green and red figures corrected in review round 1, log R-6). D7 and D8 are switched by 2N7002 (Q2, Q3) on GPIO5 /
  GPIO6 with 2.2k gate pull-downs (R19, R21): against the 33 to 73 kOhm pull-up these GPIOs power up with, the gate
  stays at 0.21 V at most (with the 10k of A2 up to 0.79 V, a faint glow of the status LEDs during boot on some
  boards).
- Rear wall windows of the enclosure: USB-C 13.4 x 7.5 mm, stacked USB-A 15.6 x 17.3 mm, microSD 14.0 x 7.2 mm. The
  USB-A window was 15.6 x 17.0 mm up to revision A2; its far edge was moved by 0.3 mm for the flange of the Jing
  connector, whose far lip lies at about 16.2 mm above the board (`enclosure/README.md`). The front face of that
  connector ends about 2.5 to 2.85 mm beyond the PCB edge: flush with the outer wall face or up to about 0.25 mm
  proud of it. The figure is scaled from a drawing that is inconsistent (17.30 mm labelled, 17.06 mm drawn); it is
  cosmetic and the enclosure model was not changed. The shell stakes of the connector reach 1.9 to 2.4 mm beyond the
  bare side of the board, into the 4 mm gap under the top plate (log R-6).

## 6a. PCB implementation (how a board gets routed without a GUI)

**Revision A2 in one paragraph** (log A2-15 to A2-27): the flow below is unchanged for the signal nets, but the copper
that carries current or USB data is no longer left to it. `gen/prewire.py` describes the PD input, the load switch,
the buck stage, the two USB port supplies and all six USB data lines as tracks and vias relative to the pads;
`build_pcb.py place` lays them (power locked from the start, the USB lines locked after the length check), the
fan-out, the autorouter and the repair loop route everything else around them. The length measurement of item 4
below still judges the USB lines and still gates the build; the USB router rules, the serpentine tuner and the six
net-order variants of items 3, 5 and 6 are not needed any more (a hand-designed line is matched by construction and
by one explicit compensation bump). Result, the same for A2 and A3 (`hardware/outputs/routing_stats.txt`): upstream
31.79 / 31.79 mm as a coupled pair, port 1 10.76 / 10.76 mm, port 2 16.21 / 16.21 mm, skew 0.00 mm, no vias, off-path
copper 0.00 mm.

**Revision A3 in one paragraph** (log K-1 to K-10, C-11, F-5, R-1, R-2, R-19 to R-24): the copper is the copper of A2.
Placement, the 1384 track segments, the 286 vias and the zones are those of A2 in a geometric comparison of the board
files (log K-7; one track end moved by 0.33 um, and 0.28 mm2 of 4190 mm2 of zone fill differ). An independent
comparison of the Gerber files agrees, apart from refill differences of at most 0.03 mm at six places of the top
ground fill and a smaller corner radius of the CM4 socket paste apertures (0.035 instead of 0.0425 mm; log R-21). What
changed is values and part numbers, the revision text on the silkscreen and three land patterns. Two of them KiCad
10's stricter pad-to-hole test asked for (ring land of the spacers H5-H8 from 2.10 to 3.00 mm radius, the four outer
contacts of the USB-C receptacle 1.11 mm long; log K-6). The third the DRC did not report: the ground ring of the
microphones MK1 / MK2 is 0.61 to 1.04 mm radius since review round 1, 0.21 mm from the 0.8 mm non-plated sound hole
instead of 0.18 mm (log R-2). No copper of the board is now closer than 0.2 mm to a non-plated hole. The solder paste
follows the narrowed lands since the second part of review round 1 (log R-19): four apertures per spacer from 2.15 to
2.92 mm radius and six per microphone ring from 0.63 mm, and no paste lies outside a solder mask opening on either
side. That is measured in the Gerber files; no check of the build compares paste with mask. The board file carries the
title block of the schematic - revision A3, date 2026-11-21 - and the Gerber headers name the revision (log R-24). The
last value change was R41 = 75 ohm in the PDM clock (log R-1). The toolchain is KiCad 10.0.8: `hardware/build.sh` runs
in the project image `voxhub-kicad:10.0`, which it builds from `hardware/docker/Dockerfile` when the image is missing
(official `kicad/kicad:10.0` plus numpy and shapely). The generators write native KiCad 10 files and take every stock
symbol and footprint from frozen copies in `hardware/gen/stock/` (the KiCad 9.0.9 library content in KiCad 10 format,
33 symbols and 30 footprints; every footprint link is `voxhub:<name>`), so the build does not read the libraries of
the installation, and a part that is not in `gen/stock` has to be added there before a generator can use it. ERC 0
errors, 0 warnings; DRC 0 violations, 0 unconnected pads, 0 footprint errors. Every ERC and DRC check is an error,
except four keys that are ignored (log K-14): the two waived library-comparison checks, which report 4 warnings in
`outputs/drc_waived_checks_enabled.rpt` (U2 footprint type; J4, J6 and C13 against their library copies), and the two
footprint-filter checks. Via covering stays "Epoxy Filled & Capped": 294 holes of 0.30 mm (286 vias and the 8 thermal
holes of the U2 footprint), 13 of them in or at exposed pads (U1 2, U2 8, U4 2, U10 1). The board is built from the
committed autorouter session (`SKIP_ROUTE=1`); a fresh autoroute from the DSN file KiCad 10 writes, and anything on
Windows, was not tried (log K-10).

**Editing the project by hand in KiCad 10** (milestone 3, log K-11 to K-15, as corrected by review round 1, log R-8
to R-18 and R-30 to R-34): generated files and manual edits are kept apart. `gen/guard.py` records the SHA-256 sum
of every generated project file in `gen/generated.sha256` (42 lines: the six sheets, the symbol library, the 33
footprints, the board, the project file; CR LF counts as LF), each sum directly after the generator command that
wrote the file. A generating step (`build.sh sch`, `pcb`, `route`, `all`) compares the files with that record before
its first generator command and again before every further one. It stops and names the reason in four cases: a file
was changed, added or deleted; a file it is going to write has no line in the record (`not recorded`, so a missing
record stops the step); the last run of the step did not finish (`unfinished`: while a step runs, the record has one
more line, `build step sch` or `build step pcb`); or KiCad has the project open (a lock file `hardware/~*.lck`).
At the first check nothing has been written yet; at a later check the files the step has already regenerated stay
as they are and the file it names is not touched. `OVERWRITE=1 hardware/build.sh <step>` discards the edits and
regenerates; only the exact value 1 counts, and it does not skip the stop for an open KiCad. `sch` checks the sheets,
the symbol library, the footprints and the project file, `pcb` and `route` check the board and the project file,
and `all` checks everything before anything is written. Ctrl-C, SIGTERM and SIGHUP end the running step and its
container (0.6 to 0.9 s in the tests), and at the end of every container call the files under `hardware/` and
`fab/` are given back to the owner of `hardware/`. `hardware/build.sh outputs` generates nothing and never reads or
writes the record: it makes the fabrication files from the project as it is on disk, gated by the DRC with schematic
parity, and writes none of the project files; its DRC run with the two waived checks switched on works on a copy
under `gen/work/waived/`. One window remains: a board or project file that is saved while a board-writing generator
command runs is lost without a message, because the lock file is looked at only at the checks. Those commands take
66.7 s of a build of 229 s (measured in one build: 24.6 s in `pcb`, 41.4 s in `route`, 0.69 s in `sch`; log R-30).
So KiCad has to be closed before a generating step.
Without Docker, `hardware/voxhub.kicad_jobset` writes the ERC and DRC reports, the schematic PDF, Gerbers, drill
files, position file and STEP into `outputs/` and the Gerber archive `fab/jlcpcb/gerbers.zip` from the KiCad 10
project manager. Its limits: it does not stop on a violation (the destination is only marked red); run with
`kicad-cli jobset run`, its DRC job skips schematic parity; the position file is named `voxhub-all-pos.csv`; BOM,
CPL, DXF, renders and the gate reports come from `build.sh outputs` only. The board file stores the plot settings of
`build.sh`, so *File > Plot* writes the same Gerbers; the drill dialog has no project settings, so drill files come
from the jobset or from `build.sh outputs`. A simplified stand-in model of the two CM4 sockets ends the
missing-model message of the 3D viewer. A change that is to stay belongs in the generator (`gen/design.py`,
`gen/build_pcb.py`, `gen/prewire.py`, `gen/project_settings.json`): BOM, order cost and the figures in the
documents come from the code model, not from an edited schematic. Tested with KiCad 10.0.8 on Linux only, in
containers and with the windows of KiCad on a virtual display under a script: by the agent that built the package,
by an independent tester (two high, one medium, eight low findings, all fixed or decided) and, after the fixes, by
a second independent tester, who found no high or medium problem. Not tested: anything on Windows, including a
checkout on a Windows drive; `KICAD_NATIVE=1` outside the KiCad container; a fresh autoroute without `SKIP_ROUTE=1`;
a save from a KiCad window while a build runs.

- 4 layers: F.Cu / GND plane / +5 V plane / B.Cu. +3V3 is routed (<= 0.5 A, widely distributed).
- **Placement by script**: fixed positions for connectors, ICs and mechanics; passives are placed greedily next to the pad they
  connect to (decoupling capacitors are spread over the supply pins of their IC). The first, tighter placement (0.15 mm between
  courtyards) left no routing channels; 0.45 mm plus a 0.6 mm halo around ICs made the board routable.
- **Power fan-out by script** before autorouting: every GND / +5 V SMD pad gets a stub + via to its plane; CM4 socket power pins use vias
  on the connector centre line so that the outside of both pad rows stays free for signal escape; the 0.4 mm WLCSP gets a fixed escape pattern.
- **Autorouter: Freerouting 2.0.1.** v2.5.0 was tried first: it honours a pass limit but did not converge on this board (30-80
  open connections, oscillating). v2.0.1 leaves 4-5 connections open and stops by itself; it ignores `--router.max_passes`, so it is
  not used with a tight placement where it never terminates. v1.9.0 needs a display (no headless JRE support).
- **Patch router + DRC-driven repair** (in `gen/build_pcb.py`): completes the remaining connections with A* and rip-up, and removes
  anything KiCad's DRC objects to, iterating until `kicad-cli pcb drc` reports nothing. KiCad's DRC is the only judge.
  Since the KiCad 10 migration the loop (`gen/postroute.sh`) counts only violations that name a track or a via as
  repairable; the final DRC gate of `build.sh outputs` is unchanged and strict (log K-8).
- **USB (revision A1)**: the hub is rotated so that its downstream pins face the USB-A connector and its upstream pins face the CM4. The six data
  nets were re-routed after everything else as 0.2 mm traces. True coupled-pair routing was not attempted in A1; A2 routes the
  upstream pair coupled by hand (the pair enters the CM4 socket pads from the centre channel, so no crossover is needed).
- **USB length matching (revision A1, redone after the review - log no. 18; superseded by the hand-designed lines of A2)**, all in `gen/build_pcb.py`:
  1. *Placement for a flow-through layout.* The two USBLC6-2SC6 sit between hub and connector, turned so that one pad row
     (4, 5, 6) faces the hub and the other (3, 2, 1) the connector, D+ on the left and D- on the right in both rows - the
     same order as the hub pins and the connector pins. Port 2 is on the left, port 1 on the right, like the hub pins.
     No line has to cross another one or change layer. (The first A1 layout had the parts turned by 90 degrees, pad
     columns left / right: whichever line was laid first cut the other one off from its pad.)
  2. *Flow-through ESD links.* The two pads of one line (1+6, 3+4) are joined by a locked straight track under the
     package (ST's flow-through layout). `ESD_ROLE` fixes which pad each side uses: 6 / 4 for the hub, 1 / 3 for the
     connector.
  3. *Router rules for the six data nets* (`patch()`): a line is always laid pad to pad from scratch (left-over copper
     of an earlier attempt is discarded first, the autorouter's USB tracks are discarded in `finish()`); the hub side
     first; the line's own copper and its other ESD pad are obstacles, so it can neither cross itself nor run over a
     pad it does not end on; a lane is kept free next to the ESD pads of the partner line; other tracks are ripped up
     before a layer change is considered; no other net may rip up USB copper. A join that cannot keep these rules
     fails, and the variant is discarded, instead of producing a stub.
  4. *Electrical length.* `usb_report()` builds the copper graph of each net and measures the shortest path from the
     hub pin to the connector pin, 1.5 mm per via. The graph joins copper wherever it really touches (crossing centre
     lines, a track over a pad or through a via land, overlapping track ends), not only at track end points. It also
     reports the skew with vias counted as 0 (the via length is an estimate), the pads the path runs through, and the
     copper of the net that is not on the path ("off-path": stubs, loops).
  5. *Serpentines.* `tune_usb()` adds 45-degree-mitred meander bumps (0.6 mm pitch = 3 x track width, amplitude
     0.3..3 mm) to the shorter line of each pair, only where the copper keeps 0.17 mm to everything else (0.4 mm to
     its own net), until the path lengths agree.
  6. `postroute.sh` tries up to six net orders and keeps the best: the score is the worst of skew, skew with vias
     counted as 0 and off-path copper. A missing measurement counts as failed, and `stats` (called by `build.sh
     outputs`) exits non-zero above 3.8 mm.

  Result of revision A1 (the A2 figures are in the paragraph at the top of this section): upstream 30.96 / 30.96 mm, port 1 11.21 / 11.21 mm, port 2
  15.17 / 15.17 mm -> skew 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00 mm; all six lines on F.Cu without a single via; path of every
  downstream line: hub pin > ESD pad > ESD pad > connector pin; off-path copper <= 0.05 mm (track ends inside pads).
  Independent re-computations (review scripts with their own board parsers, not in the repository): the first
  reviewer's script gives 30.963 / 30.963, 11.253 / 11.242 and 15.215 / 15.215 mm on the committed board, no crossings,
  no T-junctions, no duplicate USB segments. The second review round wrote a third parser for the board of commit
  81bcdf0 (30.986 / 30.986 mm upstream there). The router session after the power-stage change re-laid the upstream
  pair and moved the port 1 D+ meander by 0.5 mm, so the third round ran that script again on the committed board and
  also wrote a fourth parser: both give 30.963 / 30.963, 11.208 / 11.208, 15.170 / 15.170 mm,
  serpentines not shorted (same-net copper that is more than 1 mm apart along the line stays 0.6 mm apart), smallest
  gap to another net 0.158 mm, D+ / D- not swapped anywhere between hub, ESD parts and connectors.

  *What was wrong before:* the first A1 board reported 0.00 / 1.35 / 0.00 mm. The measurement only joined copper at
  track end points; on port 2 the hub-side track of D- crossed the connector-side track of the same net next to an ESD
  pad, the metric measured the long way round the resulting 4.9 mm loop, and the tuner matched the pair to that wrong
  length. The real skew of port 2 was 4.9 mm, above the 3.8 mm limit, and three of the four downstream lines had stubs
  of 1 .. 2.3 mm. The independent review found it by re-computing the lengths from the board file.
- Two pcbnew scripting pitfalls that cost time and are worth knowing: `BOARD.Save()` rewrites the `.kicad_pro` with default design
  rules (seen with KiCad 9; pcbnew of KiCad 10.0.8 left the project file alone in the probes of review round 1,
  log R-9; the build still copies `gen/project_settings.json` after every save), and KiCad orders tracks by random
  UUID on save, which made
  the patch router non-deterministic until tracks were sorted canonically. A third one from milestone 3:
  `pcbnew.LSET.AllCuMask()` returns a shared constant in Python; adding layers to it changes it for the whole process
  (the board writer then dropped the solder mask layers from every pad with a hole). Work on a copy (log K-14).
- The mute switch SW1 (C&K JS202011JCQN, vertical actuator) sits on the side facing the top cover; it replaced the side-actuated
  PCM12 that went end-of-life.

## 7. Operating system

- **Board ID stays `rpi4-64`.** HAOS OTA bundles are matched by board ID; a private board ID would cut the device
  off from official updates.
- HAOS's update hook deletes `/overlays/*` on the boot partition at every OS update but preserves `config.txt`.
  Therefore the default image uses **only stock overlays** (`googlevoicehat-soundcard`, `uart4`, `gpio-led`, `gpio-key`)
  configured from `config.txt` - it survives updates. The hardware pin assignment was chosen to make that possible.
- A proper single overlay (`os/overlay/voxhub-overlay.dts`) plus a Buildroot board definition is provided for
  from-source builds; it was compiled and applied against the real `bcm2711-rpi-cm4.dtb` of HAOS 18.5.
- **Full build (second session).** `build-image.sh full` now refuses to run as root (HAOS's `enter.sh` and Buildroot do
  too), points Buildroot's download and ccache directories away from `/cache` (they only exist inside the HAOS builder
  container) and can fetch GitHub "archive" sources with git (`VOXHUB_GITHUB_VIA_GIT=1`): every such tarball that
  Buildroot's own mirror does not have is produced with `git fetch --depth 1` + `git archive | gzip -n`, which is
  byte-identical to GitHub's tarball, and handed to Buildroot as `BR2_PRIMARY_SITE`. Buildroot's hash check stays in force.
  It can also use a Go module proxy (`VOXHUB_GOPROXY`), builds `host-libyaml` first on hosts with `libyaml-dev` and
  redirects the `/build/key.pem` paths of the HAOS scripts on host builds. With these the full build ran to the end in
  this environment (ninth attempt); the run log with every stop and its cause is in os/README.md.
- The radio's reset line is active high since revision A1 (GPIO24 high = reset); nothing in the OS configuration
  drives it, the flashing instructions in hardware/README.md were updated.
- The mute switch is read on GPIO27 with the stock `gpio-key` overlay (high = muted, input event KEY_MICMUTE). Since
  review round 1 the internal pull-down of that input is switched on: `gpio_pull=down` in
  `os/config/config-voxhub.txt` (it was off) and `brcm,pull = <1>` in `os/overlay/voxhub-overlay.dts`. The external
  100k alone left 1.01 V at the pin in the worst case of input leakage, above the 0.8 V low threshold. With the
  pull-down the input sees at most 0.42 V unmuted and about 2.5 V muted, 2.21 V in the worst case against the 2.0 V
  high threshold (section 4, log R-3). The hardware mute does not depend on it.
- The second board of the two-device system, the satellite, runs Raspberry Pi OS Lite with the same `config.txt`
  block (`os/config/config-voxhub.txt`); its image is built by `os/satellite` (log S-19 to S-27).

## 8. Voice assistant

- Self-contained Home Assistant add-on (`assistant/voxhub_assistant`): wake word (openWakeWord via Wyoming) ->
  VAD -> STT (Whisper add-on via Wyoming) -> Claude Messages API with tool use against the Home Assistant API ->
  TTS (Piper add-on via Wyoming) -> speaker. Only text is sent to Anthropic; audio stays on the device.
- Default model `claude-haiku-5-5` (latency), selectable `claude-sonnet-5-5` / `claude-opus-5-5`.
- Dangerous domains (`lock`, `alarm_control_panel`) are never controllable by default.
- Since version 0.1.1 the capture path removes a constant level before the gain (`DcBlocker` in `audio.py`: one-pole
  high-pass, 12.7 Hz corner at 16 kHz), because the right-channel microphone of revision A3 adds one (log W-1).
  The filter works through long buffers in blocks of at most 4096 samples, rejects a pole outside 0..1 and applies
  the microphone gain before the single rounding to 16 bit (log R-4). Version 0.1.1 is not released yet.
- The default microphone gain (`mic_gain`) is 32 since review round 1 (log R-27); the range is 0.1 to 64. The
  end-pointer counts a 20 ms frame as speech only above 120 counts rms after the gain, and speech of 60 dBSPL at the
  microphones is about 7.3 counts rms before it. Calculated, the floor is reached at about 84 / 66 / 54 / 48 dBSPL
  at gain 1 / 8 / 32 / 64. In a simulation with recorded speech through the add-on's own code, requests were
  recorded from about 65 dBSPL at gain 16, 60 dBSPL at gain 32 and 55 dBSPL at gain 64, and the highest samples of
  speech reached full scale at about 80 to 90 dBSPL at gain 32. With the earlier default of 1.0 the add-on heard the
  wake word, beeped and then logged `No speech heard`. An installation that was configured with an earlier version
  may keep its stored gain (log R-35). The value has to be calibrated on the first board (open item 11).
- Tests: 144 pytest tests, 93 for the VoxHub Assistant add-on and 51 for the VoxHub Satellite add-on; ruff is clean.
  They cover the filter class, the real `Microphone` reader (a constant level is gone before the gain, also after a
  restart) and the pipeline with its noise tracker (`test_pipeline.py`, 7 tests). Two of these tests put a 200 Hz
  tone on a constant level of 166 counts. One tone has 354 counts rms before the gain and runs at gain 4, about the
  level of a 94 dBSPL tone: it is found with the filter, and without the filter the pipeline times out. The other
  has 7.3 counts rms (60 dBSPL): it is not recorded at gain 8 and is recorded at the default gain; this test accepts
  any default from 17 to 64 (log R-37). None of it ran on the board.
- This add-on is the single-device alternative. The two-device system (hub and satellite) uses Home Assistant's own
  Assist pipeline with Linux Voice Assistant on both boards and must not run next to this add-on (log S-1 to S-11).

## 9. Footprint / pin-out verification against the datasheets

Checked on 2026-11-11 against the manufacturer documents below (pin-out against the pin table, footprint against the
recommended land pattern or package drawing, strap / component values against the application section). Where the
manufacturer's own server refused the download, the same manufacturer PDF was read from a mirror; that is stated.
"Deviation" rows say what was found and what was done about it.
Entries marked **A3** were added on 2026-11-20: the documents of every part whose value or part number changed in
revision A3 were fetched again and read by reviewers that had not made the change (Revision A3 log, entries C, N and
F). They say what was verified and what the documents do not publish. Review round 1 of 2026-11-21 fetched these
documents once more and corrected several A3 entries (log R-1 to R-6); the rows carry the corrected text.

| Part | Source (revision) | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Raspberry Pi CM4 (pin-out, mechanics, antenna) | https://datasheets.raspberrypi.com/cm4/cm4-datasheet.pdf (Release 4, 30/06/2026); official CM4IO KiCad files https://datasheets.raspberrypi.com/cm4io/CM4IO-KiCAD.zip | **verified against datasheet** - 200 / 200 pin names (five spelling-only differences), all used nets, socket spacing, module outline, H5-H8, viewing side (all 200 pads coincide with the official CM4IO footprint). **Deviation found and fixed:** antenna keep-out was in the wrong half of the edge (log no. 1). **A3 (the same release read again on 2026-11-20 for electrical values, with the CM4 IO board datasheet of 2022-06-07, https://datasheets.raspberrypi.com/cm4io/cm4io-datasheet.pdf):** GPIO5 / GPIO6 power up with a pull-up and GPIO25 with a pull-down of 33 / 47 / 73 kOhm (tables 1 and 4) - gate pull-downs R19 / R21 now 2.2k (log C-7), strap pull-up R45 confirmed at 2.2k; GPIO inputs: low level up to 0.8 V, high level from 2.0 V, leakage at most 10 uA (table 4) - with it the mute sense input GPIO27, held low only by R24 = 100k, could sit at 1.01 V unmuted, so its internal pull-down is switched on since review round 1: at most 0.42 V unmuted, 2.21 V in the worst case muted (log R-3); 3.3 V output 3.3 V +-2.5 %, 600 mA in total; pin 21 (activity LED) is active low, 20 mA maximum, VOL below 0.4 V, and the IO board drives a green LED through 1k from 3.3 V as this board does. Not published: what drives pin 21 and its VOL at about 1 mA, so no minimum current can be given for D10 (log C-6); a waveform showing nEXTRST low during the whole 3.3 V ramp; the behaviour of the 3.3 V regulator above 600 mA. |
| CM4 socket Hirose DF40C-100DS-0.4V(51) | Hirose DF40 catalogue, edition 2013.11, via mirror https://cdn-shop.adafruit.com/product-files/2227/ed_DF40_20140305-337786.pdf; **A2: re-read from hirose.com** (catalogue Jan. 2025, drawing EDC3-311352-00, guideline) - strips and paste aperture now follow it, log A2-24 / A2-25 | **verified against datasheet** - land pattern, 1.5 mm mated height, 1.5 mm spacer. Note: the inward power stubs cross Hirose's "layout prohibited area" between the pad rows exactly as on the official CM4IO board. |
| CYPD3177-24LQXQ | https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/24/49/infineon-cypd3177-24lqxq-datasheet-en.pdf (002-25383 Rev. *C, 2024-09-18); kit guide 002-25263 Rev. *B https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/24/44/infineon-cy4533-ez-pd-barrel-connector-replacement-evk-guide-usermanual-en.pdf | **verified against datasheet** - pin-out, QFN-24 land pattern, active-low P-FET driver, FLIP 49.9k, I2C 0x08, unused pins, PDO selection algorithm. **A3 (both documents fetched again on 2026-11-20):** the strap resistors of revision A3 (R1 / R2 = 12k / 2.2k, R3 = 1k, R4 / R5 = 2.2k / 1k, R6 = 1k) are verified against the pin voltage windows of tables 2 to 4 ("Voltage range on pin": 0-248 / 249-786 / 787-1347 / 1348-1920 / 1921-2778 / from 2779 mV at VDDD = 3.3 V), not against the resistor columns, which are headed "suggested": 503-520 mV for 9 V, 1017-1046 mV for 2 A, a single 1k for each end level (input leakage 2 nA; Infineon's kit also sets end levels with single resistors) - log C-2. Review round 1 recomputed every strap independently: R1 / R2 gives 511 mV with margins of 254 / 266 mV to the window limits, R4 / R5 1031 mV with a smallest margin of 230 mV (log R-6). Also verified: a FLIP pull-up of 50 kOhm means "port not data capable"; VDDD capacitor at least 0.8 uF "X5R ceramic or better" plus 2 x 100 nF and 1 uF on VCCD, met by the new 1 uF 50 V X7R part (0.98 uF at 3.3 V); driver data of VBUS_FET_EN (drives to 0 V or VBUS, pull-down at most 3 kOhm) for the gate network of Q1 (log C-3). Not published, so not verified: when the straps are sampled and against which reference; the current rating of the internal 3.3 V regulator that feeds the dividers (they draw 1.26 mA continuously, and about 2.7 mA flow while FAULT is high, because the red LED D4 is fed from the FAULT pin through R7 = 1k; dividers and FLIP pull-up of the datasheet's own example circuit draw 1.9 mA); drive mode and soft start of VBUS_FET_EN; how the FLIP pull-up is measured. **Deviation found and fixed (A1):** VDDD decoupling (1 uF X7R 0603 + 2 x 100 nF instead of 1 uF X5R 0402 + 100 nF). Note: the single-FET gate network differs from the two-FET reference circuit - measure VGS on the prototype at the contract of each test charger. Expected: 4.2 to 4.8 V at 9 V, 5.6 to 6.4 V at 12 V, 6.9 to 8.0 V at 15 V, 9.0 to 10.6 V at 20 V (there the lower values mean that D5 is clamping, which is normal). |
| LMR33640ADDA | https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/lmr33640.pdf (SNVSB99C, rev. November 2020) | **verified against datasheet** - pin-out, HSOIC-8 PowerPAD land pattern, "A" = 400 kHz / 4 A, CBOOT, CVCC; L = 6.8 uH and COUT = 4 x 22 uF are the table row for 5 V / 400 kHz. **A3 (fetched again on 2026-11-20, with the EVM guide SNVU664):** EN divider R11 / R12 = 100k / 20k from the EN threshold 1.200 / 1.231 / 1.260 V and 100 mV hysteresis: on at 7.08 / 7.39 / 7.69 V, off at 6.49 / 6.79 / 7.08 V (A2: 470k / 100k, 7.02 V / 6.45 V typical); the lower resistor is inside TI's 10 to 100 kOhm range; the regulator is off before it runs out of input voltage at 3.45 A (5.85 to 6.01 V); EN at most 3.64 V at 21.5 V in (log C-4). Under load on a 9 V contract the highest turn-off (7.08 V; 7.26 V if the hysteresis is 70 mV) meets the lowest valid sink voltage, 7.30 V at the socket minus 0.2 to 0.3 V in Q1 and copper: no margin at the theoretical peak, 0.34 V or more at 12 W, about 0.75 V in the static corner; with a long or thin cable (from about 0.5 ohm loop resistance) a restart loop is possible, which is not damage (log C-4, R-6). Feedback 100k / 24.9k: 4.86 / 5.02 / 5.17 V, up to 5.23 V at light load. CVCC as the 1 uF 50 V X7R part (0.89 uF at 5 V; log C-5). Loss of the regulator at 3.45 A from the efficiency curves: 1.34 W at 9 V in, 1.63 W at 20 V in. The curves are taken at 25 C ambient, a junction of about 70 C; at the junction temperature the thermal model predicts, Fig. 9-4 gives about 1.6 to 1.9 W. Junction rating 125 C, absolute maximum 150 C, thermal shutdown at 165 C (on again at 148 C); the model puts the junction at about 150 to 165 C at the theoretical peak and at about 100 to 113 C at 12 W (log N-2, R-5). Worst-case inductor peak 4.51 A against the 4.8 A minimum current limit (log N-4). Not verified: the EN hysteresis is published as a typical value only; no loop-gain data is published, so the phase margin with the fitted output capacitors (54 uF effective at the regulator, 109 uF on the rail) cannot be calculated; the losses at 9 V and 20 V are interpolated between TI's curves for 8 / 12 / 24 V; the layout text asks for a 4 x 4 array of thermal vias and 2 oz outer copper, the board has the 8 vias of TI's own land pattern drawing (drilled 0.3 mm) and 1 oz outer copper. **Deviations found and fixed (A1):** RFBB 24.3k -> 24.9k, CHF 100 nF -> 220 nF X7R, CIN X5R -> X7R (log no. 2, 3). |

The separate land-pattern document 90-0031 could not be retrieved. **A3 (2026-11-20):** Rev 11 (5/18) read from the LCSC copy of C910544 and cross-checked with Rev 7; analog.com still refuses. Read from the curves for 5 V and 4 ohm: about 86 % efficiency at 3.2 W (0.53 W loss), 82 % at 1 W (log N-3). | ADAU7002ACBZ | ADI ADAU7002 Rev. C (11/2019), original PDF via mirror https://cdn.eicom.ru/media/PDF/2429992.pdf | **verified against datasheet** - ball map, CONFIG = IOVDD -> I2S, 1.62-3.6 V, BCLK = 64 fs, 0.4 mm ball grid. The datasheet gives no land pattern (pad diameter 0.22 mm stays an IPC choice). **A3 (2026-11-20; Rev. C again, from the mirror and from the LCSC copy of C481886 - analog.com still refuses):** frequency response "DC to 0.45 output fS" and no high-pass filter, so a constant level in a microphone's PDM stream reaches the I2S samples (log F-3, W-1); 64x decimation, 20-bit words; set-up 10 ns / hold 7 ns against the microphone timing (data valid at most 100 ns and not before 40 ns after the clock edge at the microphone). Margins, calculated for R41 = 75 ohm: set-up about 41.5 ns with the nominal load of the clock model of log R-1 and about 39 ns in its slow corner; hold at least 33 ns. The first calculation, made for 33 ohm, had given 43.5 ns and 33 ns. Not published: output resistance, edge rate and duty cycle of PDM_CLK; the microphone asks for 48 to 52 % and edges of at most 13 ns, and the choice of R41 rests on simulations with assumed driver data: 75 ohm since review round 1, 33 ohm before (log R-1, C-8). How the margins are calculated: set-up = 156.25 ns (the shorter half period at 3.072 MHz and 48 % duty) - 100 ns - 10 ns - 2.0 ns for 283 mm of clock and data track at an assumed 7 ps/mm - the delay of the clock edge behind R41, taken as the time one RC stage needs to reach 65 % of the rail (the VIH minimum of the microphone), 1.05 x (R41 + driver resistance) x load. The load is that of the clock model, all assumed values: 137.4 mm of clock track at 0.115 pF/mm and two inputs of 5 pF, together 25.8 pF, behind 75 + 25 ohm: 2.7 ns; in the slow corner 0.14 pF/mm (derived from the 43.6 ohm of the track at its tolerance corner) and two inputs of 10 pF, together 39.2 pF, behind 75 + 50 ohm: 5.1 ns. Hold = 40 ns - 7 ns; every delay of the clock or of the data adds to it. The same calculation with 33 ohm gives 42.7 and 40.9 ns, so the 75 ohm cost 1.1 to 1.7 ns of about 40 ns. Not measured. |

With that model Q1 is above its 150 C limit at the theoretical peak on a 9 V contract and at about 100 to 120 C at 12 W; it has no thermal protection (log N-2, R-5). |
