| Microchip USB2514BT-I/M2 (U4) | the datasheet of the USB2514B-I/M2 row in the first table (DS00001692E), p. 55 "Product Identification System" | **verified against datasheet** - "T = Tape and Reel"; the identifier "is used for ordering purposes and is not printed on the device package": same device, package (36-pin SQFN) and temperature range as the USB2514B-I/M2. JLCPCB's footprint of C510949 has the pad numbers and the pin-1 corner of the one of C220679, the CPL angle is unchanged (log F-1) |
| Infineon IM72D128V01 (MK1, MK2; ordered as IM72D128V01XTMA1) | https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/30/49/infineon-im72d128-datasheet-en.pdf (V1.01, 2022-05-05), compared with IM69D130 V1.0 (2017-12-19) and IM73D122V01 V1.00 (2022-08-12) from the same server | **verified against datasheet** - package 4.0 x 3.0 x 1.2 mm (PG-LLGA-5-3), pads, pin functions (1 DATA, 2 VDD, 3 CLOCK, 4 SELECT, 5 GND), 0.6 mm port and recommended land pattern with the 0.8 mm board hole are those of the IM69D130 (package PG-LLGA-5-1), so the footprint was kept (its pads are 0.70 mm long, all three sheets draw 0.75 mm; its ground ring runs from 0.61 to 1.04 mm radius since review round 1, where the sheet draws 0.58 to 1.04 mm, so that the copper keeps 0.21 mm to the non-plated hole - log R-2); JLCPCB's footprint records of the two parts have identical pads; VDD 1.62 to 3.6 V, absolute maximum 3.6 V on any pin (the IM69D130: 4.0 V; rail at most 3.38 V), -40 to +85 C; no input hysteresis is specified (the IM69D130 sheet gives 0.1 to 0.29 x VDD); 3.072 MHz lies in the 2.9 to 3.3 MHz clock window, which is the same as that of the IM69D130, while the other clock windows are narrower (the sound card must stay at 48 kHz, which the googlevoicehat codec driver enforces); duty cycle 48 to 52 %, edges at most 13 ns; data timing against the ADAU7002 unchanged (see its row); SELECT = GND is the left channel, SELECT = VDD the right; sensitivity -36 dBFS +-1 dB, SNR 72 dB(A), overload point 128 dBSPL; a 1 uF capacitor "shall be placed close to the microphone VDD pad to ensure best SNR performance" (table footnote; a note on p. 10 of the same sheet still says 100 nF) - C43 / C44 are 1 uF; constant level in the data stream -80 dBFS with SELECT = GND (MK1) and -40 dBFS with SELECT = VDD (MK2), typical values (section 3.4, table 7). Not verified: spread and sign of the constant level (only typical values are given); duty cycle and overshoot of the clock at the microphone pins. Simulations with assumed driver and input data give, with R41 = 75 ohm, a peak of 3.41 V nominal and 3.53 / 3.54 V at MK1 / MK2 in the fast corner at a 3.30 V rail, and 3.62 / 3.63 V at the 3.3825 V top of the rail tolerance without track loss (3.55 V with it); the limit is 3.6 V. The value of the first A3 build gave 3.81 / 3.94 V in the same fast corner, which is why R41 was changed (log R-1). Nothing was measured on hardware (log F-3, W-1) |
| LEDs Hubei KENTO KT-0603R (D4, D9), KT-0603G (D6, D7, D10), KT-0603B (D8) | "Specification for approval", Revision A.0 (2018-12-06) of each part, LCSC copies https://datasheet.lcsc.com/datasheet/pdf/011ec3e8cb1e825f6961d29bc4db4c7a.pdf (C2286), https://datasheet.lcsc.com/datasheet/pdf/69e84a3abd84e5ab5856c47a2d0334ce.pdf (C12624), https://datasheet.lcsc.com/datasheet/pdf/1096dc34dcf507e4677a7d79542be828.pdf (C2288); compared with Lite-On LTST-C191KRKT DS22-2000-223 Rev. F | **verified against datasheet** - KT-0603R: 25 mA, 40 mW, 145 to 300 mcd and 1.8 to 2.4 V at 20 mA, derating to 6.7 mA at 85 C (the board loads it with at most 1.86 mA); KT-0603G: 30 mA, 210 to 430 mcd and 2.6 to 3.1 V at 5 mA; KT-0603B: 30 mA, 70 to 175 mcd and 2.6 to 3.1 V at 5 mA; all -40 to +85 C. Currents and intensities of log C-6 are computed from the typical curves and the bin limits (since review round 1: green about 10 to 55 mcd at 0.27 mA, red about 11 to 27 mcd at 1.4 mA, blue 23 to 57 mcd). JLCPCB's footprint of C2286 has pad 1 = anode, those of C12624 and C2288 pad 1 = cathode; the placement report shows D4 and D9 as "terminals swapped by pin name" with unchanged rotations. Polarity mark, read in review round 1 on page 2 of all three sheets (log R-25): terminal 1 is the anode ("+"), terminal 2 the cathode; the cathode end carries a green mark on the top face, and the underside shows a green T-shaped mark. On the board the cathode is pad 1 of all six LEDs, at the closed end of the white bracket of the silkscreen. Not verified: the curves start at about 1.1 mA and the intensity curves cannot be read below about 0.1 mA, so the values at 0.27 mA (D6, D7) and for D10 are extrapolated; KENTO gives no typical intensity; a minimum current for D10 cannot be derived from published limits |
| Samsung CL10B105KB8NQNC, 1 uF 50 V X7R 0603 (C1, C2, C3, C5, C11) | Samsung "Specification (Reference sheet)" CL10B105KB8NQNC (PDF of 2022-10-17, LCSC copy https://datasheet.lcsc.com/datasheet/pdf/d6d93098a36e9601e7f96915ab74f302.pdf) and the characteristic data in Samsung's component library, https://weblib.samsungsem.com/mlcc/mlcc-ec-data-sheet.do?partNumber=CL10B105KB8NQN | **verified against datasheet** - 1 uF, 50 V, +-10 %, X7R (-55 to +125 C), 1.60 x 0.80 x 0.80 mm on the 0603 pads of the three parts it replaces. Typical capacitance under DC bias: -1.6 % at 3.3 V, -10.7 % at 5 V (0.89 uF), -69.3 % at 20 V (0.31 uF); the replaced 25 V X7R part kept 0.83 uF at 5 V, the replaced 50 V X5R part 0.15 uF at 20 V. Checked with these values: CYPD3177 VDDD (at least 0.8 uF) and VCCD, LMR33640 VCC ("1-uF, 16-V"), VBUS bypass at attach (1.78 uF with C1 + C2; USB: 1 to 10 uF) - log C-5. Not verified: the bias figures are typical curves at 25 C, not guaranteed limits; the alternates named in the BOM (Yageo, Fenghua) have no published bias curve |
| Samsung CL05A105KA5NQNC, 1 uF 25 V X5R 0402 (C43, C44) | characteristic data in Samsung's component library, https://weblib.samsungsem.com/mlcc/mlcc-ec-data-sheet.do?partNumber=CL05A105KA5NQN ; ratings from JLCPCB's record of C52923 | **verified against datasheet** - typical capacitance under DC bias -27.1 % at 3.3 V (0.73 uF), ESR 10.6 mOhm at 1 MHz; the 10 V and 16 V parts of the same size lose more than half at 3.3 V (0.45 to 0.47 uF remain), so the BOM names as alternates only parts rated 25 V or more (log R-6). It sits on the 0402 pads of the 100 nF part it replaces and is there because the microphone datasheet asks for 1 uF at VDD (log F-3). Not verified: typical curves only; the microphone datasheet gives no SNR figure for 100 nF against 1 uF |
| Jing Extension 907-112A1022D10200 (J6, stacked USB-A) | maker's drawing 907-112A1022D10200, version A0, 2021/03/10, one page (LCSC copy https://datasheet.lcsc.com/datasheet/pdf/289ecaf1808b1a422bdd31b7682f44fe.pdf); compared with the Wurth drawing rev M and with the board file | **verified against datasheet** - pin assignment, pin pitch 2.5 / 2.0 / 2.5 mm, row spacing 2.60 mm (board 2.62 mm), pin holes 8 x 0.92 mm and stake holes 2.30 mm as drilled, shell stakes 13.10 x 5.80 mm (board 13.14 x 5.68 mm: the front stakes sit 0.12 mm forward), no locating peg; shell 13.10 mm wide, 15.60 mm high, 17.30 mm deep, flange 14.60 x 15.70 mm; 1.5 A per contact, 30 V, 1500 cycles; JLCPCB has a footprint record, fitted by pad numbers with 0.045 mm at the worst pad. That record is neither exactly the land pattern of the board nor the one of the Jing drawing: its pin rows are 2.71 mm apart (board 2.62 mm, drawing 2.60 mm), which is the 0.045 mm per row, and its pin holes are 0.91 mm (log R-23; this row had said that the record carries the Wurth land pattern). It confirms position, rotation and pin 1 of J6, and the fit of the real part rests on the Jing drawing alone. **Scaled from the drawing, not dimensioned:** (1) the distance between the stake rows and the pin rows (0.35 mm; the 0.12 mm offset and the clearance of the stakes in the smallest drilled hole depend on it: 0.0 to 0.1 mm at nominal dimensions, depending on how the part centres itself, which is less than the part tolerance of +-0.25 mm and the hole position tolerance of +-0.075 mm, so the stakes, which are spring legs, may need light pressure), (2) the distance from the pin rows to the front face (front face about 2.5 to 2.85 mm beyond the PCB edge: flush with the outer wall or up to about 0.25 mm proud), (3) the heights of the flange lips (0.45 and 16.12 to 16.20 mm above the board) and of the port openings, (4) the stake cross-section (1.87 x 0.30 mm at the root), the stake length (the stakes reach 1.9 to 2.4 mm beyond the bare side of the board) and the pin width (0.58 mm). Not stated on the drawing: temperature range, solder process, flammability class. Labels and drawn geometry of the sheet differ in places (13.10 labelled, 13.15 drawn; 17.30 labelled, 17.06 drawn), and no sample was measured (log F-2, R-6) |

## 10. Sourcing (JLCPCB / LCSC live check 2026-11-21; DigiKey 2026-11-11 as the second source)

**Revision A3:** the board is assembled by JLCPCB, so the stock that counts is JLCPCB's assembly library. Every BOM
line was read live from that library and from lcsc.com (`gen/jlc_lookup.py refresh`, raw data in
`gen/sourcing_jlc.json`). The check of all lines of revision A2 ran on 2026-11-20 between 22:23 and 22:46 UTC and led
to the decisions F-1 to F-4. All 44 lines and two of the three replaced parts (not the tray part of the hub,
C220679, last read on 2026-11-20) were read again on 2026-11-21 at 09:57 UTC in review
round 1, after R41 had changed; the file, the BOM and the order cost hold that reading, and every figure of this
paragraph and of the A3 column below comes from it. `hardware/outputs/bom.csv` has 46 lines - 44 assembled and 2 not
populated (C13, J5) - and 136 placements, 132 on the component side and 4 on the side that faces the top cover. The
parts of one board cost 39.84 USD at JLCPCB's single-piece prices and 29.30 USD at its 100-piece prices (A2 on
2026-11-12: 41.78 and 30.03 USD). Of the 44 assembled lines 12 are Basic, 2 are Preferred Extended (49.9k and 75 ohm)
and 30 are Extended; 42 part types are placed by machine and 2 are soldered by hand (J6, J7). For the order of two
boards `hardware/outputs/order_cost.csv` gives 79.68 USD for the parts at the needed quantities, 83.22 USD with
JLCPCB's minimum quantities and 84.99 USD with minimum and loss quantities. These sums do not contain JLCPCB's special
fee for J1 (USB4105-GF-A, 0.03 USD per piece); `fab/ORDER_GUIDE.md` counts those 0.06 USD on a line of its own.
Against the read of 2026-11-20, whose last part was taken right after the part changes, JLCPCB's USD prices are about
0.24 % higher on almost every line (exchange rate), and the parts of one board cost 0.09 USD more. The prices of
JLCPCB's order form (PCB, assembly fees) were read on 2026-11-20, the shipping prices on 2026-11-20 and on
2026-11-21 between 00:30 and 01:00 UTC; none of them was read again with the parts at 09:57 UTC. Two lines are below 200
pieces, ADAU7002ACBZ-R7 (63) and RF-BM-2652P2 (126); neither has a stocked alternate (log F-4). No line is below twice
the need. `hardware/outputs/bom.csv` carries, per line, the LCSC number, library type, JLCPCB and LCSC stock, JLCPCB
prices at 1 / 10 / 100, the DigiKey second source and other alternates.

Not swapped although a cheaper or in-stock candidate exists - because a rating would go down, or (TPS2553) because the
in-stock alternative is the identical device on another reel and only changes the order code, or (10 uF) because the only
equal part in stock is a 2,000-piece Marketplace item:

- **2N7002,215** (0 in stock): 2N7002BK,215 is in stock and 0.3 cent dearer, but VGS is +-20 V instead of +-30 V.
- **22 uF 16 V X5R 1206, CL31A226KOHNNNE** (0 in stock): the TDK part in stock is +-20 % instead of +-10 %, the Samsung
  part in stock is only 0.85 mm thick (worse DC bias).
- **TPS2553DBVR** (0 in stock until mid-December): AP22653W6-7 is a third of the price but only rated 3.0-5.5 V and
  Diodes AP2553W6-7 is NRND. **TPS2553DBVT is the identical device on the 250-piece reel (296-23990-1-ND, 2,503 in
  stock, +0.16 USD each): order that if boards are built before the DBVR reel is back.** The BOM keeps the DBVR order
  code because it is the cheaper one for production.
- **10 uF 50 V X7R 1206, CL31B106KBHNNNE** (0 in stock): the only equal-rating part DigiKey can ship is Cal-Chip
  GMC31X7R106K50NT, a Marketplace item with a 2,000-piece minimum (0.403 USD each, separate shipping) - fine for
  production, useless for prototypes. TDK C3216X7R1H106K160AC is expected in February 2027. For a prototype the X5R part
  of revision A (CL31A106KBHNNNE) can be fitted; it loses more capacitance under bias and at temperature.
- **0603 green LED**: Kingbright APT1608CGCK is 2.7 cents cheaper but has a narrower viewing angle.
- **LMR33640ADDAR** (0 in stock, 26 weeks): no pin-compatible 4 A / 400 kHz part exists; the 1 MHz version
  LMR33640DDDAR is in stock and would need L1 = 3.3 uH (same footprint) - documented as plan B, not applied.

Cost per unit (DigiKey, 2026-11-11): see the cost summary in README.md.

## 11. Showcase animation (`/media`)

- Status: the committed video and still show revision A1. They were not rendered again for revision A2 (log A2-53)
  and not for revision A3; A3 changes little that a render shows (the revision text, three land patterns, the model
  of the USB-A connector and its window). For A3 the texture script was ported to KiCad 10, which plots a page that is
  exactly the board outline, and run on the A3 board; Blender and the encoding were not run (`media/README.md`). The
  points below describe the A1 render.
- Source geometry is only what the repository already contains as STEP: the KiCad board export
  (`hardware/outputs/voxhub_pcb.step`, placement of every part), the part models in `hardware/3dmodels` and the
  enclosure assembly (`enclosure/voxhub_assembly.step`). `step_to_glb.py` converts them with OpenCascade (XCAF reader ->
  glTF writer), keeping names and colours; Blender has no STEP importer of its own.
- KiCad's STEP export drops the colours of the part models. The Blender script therefore takes position and reference
  designator of each part from the board export and the coloured geometry from the part's own STEP model.
- The board surface (copper, solder mask, pads, silkscreen) is a texture composed from KiCad layer plots
  (`make_pcb_texture.py`); the STEP board is a plain slab.
- Choreography: the device is assembled upside down in reality (the PCB hangs under the top plate), so after the base
  plate has dropped away the upper half turns over and the PCB lifts out upwards, component side visible.
- Renderer: Cycles on the CPU, 4 samples per pixel + OpenImageDenoise, fixed noise seed, 3 bounces. EEVEE was tried
  first; without a GPU it runs through Mesa's software rasteriser and needs about 100 s per frame.
- The title is a text object parented to the camera (rendered, not composited), so the Blender script alone
  reproduces the video frame by frame.
- Encoding: H.264 High, yuv420p, CRF 17, converted with the BT.709 matrix and tagged as BT.709 limited range. The first
  encode used ffmpeg's defaults (BT.601 matrix, no tags), which HD players decode as BT.709 - the board came out a
  darker green than rendered. Found by the review, re-encoded from the same frames.
- The first render of the video was done before the review fixes; 53 of the 151 footprints have moved since (the buck
  power stage, the two USB ESD parts, small parts around the PD controller and the hub) and the board was re-routed.
  The still image and the 866 frames of the video that show the PCB (frames 150 to 1015: from the moment the device
  lifts off until it is closed again) were rendered again from the final board, 1 h 54 min. The 394 frames before and
  after show only the closed device and were kept: shell and base did not change (the STL files are byte-identical),
  the frame-to-frame difference across the two joins is the same as between their neighbours (no jump), and five of
  the kept frames (75, 1016, 1130, 1200, 1255) rendered again from the final board differ from the kept ones by at
  most 3 of 255 in single pixels. The committed MP4 is the encode of that frame set.
- `build_showcase.sh` checks its tools before the long render, runs Blender with `--python-exit-code` and refuses to
  encode while a frame is missing; a frame only counts as rendered when the PNG is complete (IEND chunk). Blender 5 is
  refused: the script uses the pre-5.0 animation API and was only run with 4.5.16.

Still open (state of revision A3, 2026-11-21):

1. **Thin stock at JLCPCB on 2026-11-21:** ADAU7002ACBZ-R7 (63; 104 on 2026-11-12) and RF-BM-2652P2 (126). Neither has
   a stocked alternate (log F-4): buy them into the JLCPCB parts library before ordering (`fab/ORDER_GUIDE.md`).
   Stock moves fast - USB2514B-I/M2 went from 1,182 to 2 pieces, IM69D130V01 from 392 to 43 and 61400826021 from 116
   to 18 in eight days - so run `gen/jlc_lookup.py refresh` again on the day of the order.
2. **Documents the manufacturer still does not serve to scripts** (read from authorised distributors or mirrors
   instead; table in the A2 log, A3 entries in section 9): MGM240P (moot), C&K JS, ADAU7002 (Rev. C), MAX98357A (Rev 11
   and Rev 13 read, Rev 16 exists), USBLC6-2 (Rev 5 read), SMBJ, 2N7002 (Rev. 7), and on 2026-11-20 also the Wurth
   drawing of the USB-A alternate (rev M read instead of Rev. 001.001). The KENTO LED sheets and the Jing drawing were
   read as LCSC copies; Microchip's hardware design check list for the hub could not be fetched. Generic passives,
   diodes, the pin header and the Tag-Connect pads use KiCad standard footprints and were not checked against a
   manufacturer document (last row of the first table in section 9).
3. **Mechanical points to check on real parts:** the plug of the Adafruit 3351 speaker (its data sheet says XH 2.54 mm,
   the shops say JST-PH 2.0 mm; J7 is PH); the C&K body tolerance; and the Jing USB-A connector 907-112A1022D10200
   (J6), of which no sample was measured. Four of its dimensions are scaled from the drawing, not dimensioned: the
   distance between the shell stakes and the pin rows (on that basis the stakes fit at nominal dimensions, 0.0 to
   0.1 mm clear in the smallest drilled hole; the part and hole tolerances are larger, so the stakes may need light
   pressure), the distance from the pins to the front face (front face about 2.5 to 2.85 mm beyond the PCB edge:
   flush with the outer wall or up to about 0.25 mm proud; the drawing is inconsistent, 17.30 mm labelled and
   17.06 mm drawn), the heights of the flange lips (0.45 and 16.12 to 16.20 mm above the board; the enclosure window
   ends at 17.0 mm) and the cross-sections of stakes and pins. The stakes reach 1.9 to 2.4 mm beyond the bare side
   of the board. JLCPCB's footprint record of the part confirms position, rotation and pin 1 only: its pin rows are
   2.71 mm apart against 2.62 mm on the board, 0.045 mm per row (log R-23), and the fit of the real part rests on
   the Jing drawing alone. The drawing states no
   temperature range, solder process or flammability class (log F-2, R-6, section 9).
4. **USB impedance is calculated, not measured.** 0.14 / 0.16 mm on JLC04161H-3313 is 89.96 ohm in JLCPCB's calculator;
   JLCPCB's tolerance without the paid impedance option is not stated. The downstream lines are two uncoupled 45 ohm
   lines per port (log A2-22).
5. **The radio flashing script has only met a simulated radio** (`tools/flash_zigbee.sh`; what was and was not tested
   is in `fab/ASSEMBLY_AND_TEST.md`). How RF-star ships the module (blank, or with a firmware whose backdoor is
   disabled) decides whether the first flash goes over the serial boot loader or over JTAG; the script handles both.
6. **JLCPCB's order form behind the login was not reachable** (BOM / CPL upload, parts matching, placement preview,
   final price), on 2026-11-20 as on 2026-11-12. Those steps of `fab/ORDER_GUIDE.md` come from JLCPCB's help
   articles, the PCB price and the shipping prices from the live form (the PCB price read again on 2026-11-20, the
   shipping prices on 2026-11-20 and on 2026-11-21 between 00:30 and 01:00 UTC; neither with the parts at 09:57
   UTC), the assembly fees from JLCPCB's price list of 22 Oct 2026. What the placement preview shows for the
   new part numbers - J6, and D4 / D9, whose JLCPCB footprint numbers its pads the other way round - has to be
   looked at in the order (log C-6, F-2). Look first at J1, J4, MK1 and MK2: for these four the fit depends on
   JLCPCB putting the origin of its footprint, not the middle of its pads, on the CPL point. The two are 0.162 mm
   apart for J1, 0.075 / 0.175 mm for J4 and 0.098 mm for the microphones; with the pad middle on the point the
   worst pad of J4 would be 0.19 mm off (log R-22).
7. Nothing here has been powered on. Treat revision A3 as a prototype spin.
8. **KiCad 10 on Windows is not verified (log K-10 to K-15, R-8 to R-18).** The evidence is KiCad 10.0.8 on Linux, in
   containers and with the windows of KiCad on a virtual display under a script, not in front of a person: an
   open-edit-save in both editors, the guard against overwriting manual edits, `build.sh outputs` on an edited
   project, the jobset and the Plot dialog. Not tried: opening the project on Windows (KiCad's setup wizard appears
   once); KiCad saving its files with CR LF there (expected from its source code; on Linux the guard and
   `build.sh outputs` accepted files converted to CR LF); the jobset on Windows; `hardware/build.sh` under WSL2 with
   Docker, which is the set-up it is meant for, or under Git Bash / Docker Desktop; `KICAD_NATIVE=1` on a machine
   with its own KiCad (in review round 1 the steps `sch`, `pcb` and `route` ran with it inside the KiCad container
   only); a fresh autoroute without `SKIP_ROUTE=1` (KiCad 10 writes the Specctra DSN file differently; the committed
   session imports unchanged); and a `docker build` of the project image without the layer cache of the machine it
   was made on. *File > Plot* now writes the Gerbers of `build.sh`, because the board file stores the plot settings.
   Take the drill files from the jobset or from `build.sh outputs`, not from the drill dialog, and use the files of
   the jobset only when its first destination is not marked red: the jobset does not stop on a DRC violation. The
   independent test of the local-editing package found two high, one medium and eight low problems, all fixed or
   decided (log R-8 to R-18), and a second independent test of the fixes found no high or medium problem (log R-30
   to R-34). Limits of the package that remain, by decision: a board or project file that is saved while a
   board-writing generator command runs is lost without a message (66.7 s of a build of 229 s; the lock file of an
   open KiCad is looked at only at the checks; log R-30), so close KiCad before `build.sh sch`, `pcb`, `route` or
   `all`; an edited sheet does not stop `pcb` or `route` (log R-14); and the ownership of the files after a build
   was tested on a Linux file system only, not on a Windows drive (log R-10).
9. **Measurements the first board has to deliver (log N-1 to N-6).** Every figure below is a calculation, a
   simulation or a model result:
   - Load switch (N-6, C-3): VGS of Q1 at the contract of each test charger. Expected: 4.2 to 4.8 V at 9 V, 5.6 to
     6.4 V at 12 V, 6.9 to 8.0 V at 15 V, 9.0 to 10.6 V at 20 V (there the lower values mean that D5 is clamping,
     which is normal). The rise of VBUS_SW and the switch-on current on a scope (simulated about 3 A at 20 V, above
     the requested 2 A for about 50 us; the drive mode of the controller is not published), FAULT staying low. Also
     record VBUS_SW and +5V on a scope when the controller opens Q1: the model gives 6 to 13 ms for the turn-off
     (up to 30 ms with the controller unpowered), and the regulator may re-enable a few times while the switch
     fades out.
   - Regulator input on a 9 V contract (C-4): on the 20 W charger with the module under load, VBUS_SW at C8 / C9 must
     stay above about 7.5 V (test 4 of `fab/ASSEMBLY_AND_TEST.md`). The regulator turns off at up to 7.08 V (7.26 V
     if its hysteresis is 70 mV); a restart loop is possible on a 9 V contract with a long or thin cable, from about
     0.5 ohm loop resistance.
   - Temperatures (N-2): regulator U2, inductor L1 and load switch Q1 in the closed enclosure at typical load and at
     about 12 W. Measure Q1 on a 9 V contract (the 20 W charger) at about 12 W, and do not hold the full peak on 9 V.
     Model for 50 C around the board, regulator: about 76 C at typical load, 100 to 113 C at 12 W, 150 to 165 C at
     the theoretical peak, where it is expected to cycle through thermal shutdown. Q1 on a 9 V contract: about 100
     to 120 C at 12 W, 115 to 150 C at 13.9 W and above its 150 C absolute maximum at the theoretical peak; on a
     15 V contract 116 to 124 C at the theoretical peak.
   - Power (N-3): input power at the connector (expected 5.2 to 5.4 W typical, about 19.6 W at the theoretical peak)
     and the 5 V rail (4.86 to 5.17 V, up to 5.23 V at light load).
   - USB-A ports (N-1, N-4, N-5): both ports with a high-speed device and under load up to the current limit
     (computed 1.19 to 1.39 A). The input capacitors and limit resistors of U5 / U6 are far from their pins, and the
     hub's only ground is two vias in its exposed pad. The limit is short-circuit protection: the power budget counts
     both ports together at about 1 A (2 x 500 mA), and with both ports at their limit the 5 V rail is asked for more
     than the regulator guarantees.
   - Inductor current (N-4): nothing to measure; do not plan loads above the 3.45 A budget at 20 V input.
10. **Brightness of the LEDs, D10 above all (log C-6).** The intensities are estimates from typical curves: green
    about 10 to 55 mcd (0.27 mA is below the range of the data sheet curve), red about 11 to 27 mcd, blue 23 to
    57 mcd. D10 (ACT, green, 1k from 3.3 V) is expected at 0.9 mA and can be at about 0.1 mA with every limit
    against it: it should light on every board, with a brightness that differs about 10 : 1 between boards, and no
    minimum current follows from published limits. Look at it on the first boards; a red KT-0603R in its place would
    have a current that the data sheet limits guarantee (0.62 to 1.86 mA).
11. **Constant level of MK2 and microphone gain on real hardware (log F-3, W-1, R-26, R-27).** The data sheet
    gives -40 dBFS as a typical value without a spread: about 328 counts in the 16-bit samples of the right channel,
    166 in the mono mix, 662 after the source volume 4 of the two-device path. The filter of the VoxHub Assistant
    add-on has unit tests and, since review round 1, tests of the capture path and of the pipeline (log R-4). The
    two-device path (Linux Voice Assistant: wake word and voice activity detection) was only simulated with library
    recordings: no difference from 60 dBSPL at the microphone, 4 to 14 percentage points fewer wake word detections
    at 45 to 55 dBSPL, voice activity detection unchanged; at eight times the typical level the wake word rate over
    45 to 60 dBSPL fell from 79 % to 49 %. The gain of both paths is calculated and simulated, not measured. On the
    first boards:
    - Record both microphones (tests 9 and S9 of `fab/ASSEMBLY_AND_TEST.md` say what MK2 sounds like). Then check
      wake word and a spoken request on the hub and on the satellite, from 1 m and from across the room: first with
      the defaults, then with a higher microphone gain, then with Mic Auto Gain and Mic Noise Suppression, which
      are set on the device page. Either of the two removes the level inside Linux Voice Assistant and also
      changes what speech recognition hears; neither is tested; switch them off again on the device page.
    - Calibrate the microphone gain of the VoxHub Assistant add-on, whose default is 32. (1) Read the source volume
      first: it must be 100 %; the VoxHub Satellite add-on leaves 4.0 behind, and 4 x 32 = 128. (2) With gain 32
      and `log_level` set to debug, speak a request in a normal voice from 1 m and from the farthest usual position,
      from straight above the microphone axis and from its side, and compare the logged length of the recording
      ("Heard x s") and the transcript with what was said. (3) Repeat with 16 and with 64 and keep the lowest gain
      that records the far request completely. (4) Speak loudly at 0.3 m and check the transcript for clipping.
      (5) Watch for a recording of a few tenths of a second with an empty transcript right after the beep: that
      would be the tail of the beep taken as speech. (6) Make a raw recording of both channels as in test 9 of
      `fab/ASSEMBLY_AND_TEST.md`, for the real constant level and the real speech level.
    - A later option, if quiet speech stays a problem in the two-device path: feed only MK1 through a remap source
      (worked on PulseAudio 17.0 in a container, needs start-up code on both devices, untested on PipeWire).
12. **PDM clock at the microphones (log R-1).** The IM72D128V01 allows 3.6 V on any pin (the IM69D130: 4.0 V), and
    its data sheet gives no hysteresis figure. With R41 = 75 ohm three independent simulations with assumed driver
    and input data agree. The authors' model gives a peak of 3.41 V nominal and 3.53 / 3.54 V at MK1 / MK2 in the
    fast corner at a 3.30 V rail. At the 3.3825 V top of the rail tolerance its fast corner is 3.62 / 3.63 V without
    track loss; the verifier's model with track loss gives 3.55 V. Edges are at most 12.0 ns (limit 13 ns). Not
    removed by 75 ohm: if the driver edge is about 0.5 ns, the rising edge at MK1 is not monotonic (a reversal of
    about 0.3 V inside the threshold band). The data sheets of the converter and of the microphone do not allow a
    calculation. Measure overshoot, edge time and duty cycle (48 to 52 %) at MK1 and MK2. That needs an oscilloscope
    of about 1 GHz and a short ground spring. Without one, watch the symptom: noise, drop-outs or a wrong level on
    one microphone channel point to the clock line. A bad result cannot be repaired on assembled boards.
13. **Solder joints of the four spacers H5-H8 (log R-19, R-28).** The Compute Module is screwed to these spacers.
    Since the paste follows the narrowed ring land, each spacer gets 10.22 mm2 of paste: 1.02 mm3 with a 0.10 mm
    stencil, 1.23 mm3 with 0.12 mm (the stencil thicknesses are assumptions). The Wurth data sheet of the spacer
    9774015151R (revision 001.002) suggests four segments from 3.8 to 5.9 mm diameter and "Solder Cream Thickness
    150 um min.", i.e. 13.26 mm2 x 0.15 mm = 1.99 mm3. The board has 51 % or 62 % of that; revision A2 had
    12.75 mm2, 64 % or 77 %. Per land area the paste is 2.6 % lower than in A2. Nothing was changed: the ring had
    to move away from the hole, the paste follows the land, and the outer edge stays at the 2.92 mm radius of the
    KiCad library footprint as in A2. All of this is calculated. Look at the joints of the four spacers on the
    first boards and add solder by hand where a fillet is missing.
